\section{Next-track: item-vector representation exploration}
\label{sec:nexttrack-representation}
\emph{Rationale.} The compression axis (Sections~\ref{sec:nexttrack-compression}
and~\ref{sec:nexttrack-dim}) found latent \emph{dimension} to dominate but never
asked whether the \emph{composition} of the 192-d item vector --- the
equal-weighted 4-block PCA (text / numeric / acoustic / categorical) --- is itself a
handicap. An explained-variance-ratio (EVR) preflight said it might be: the raw
input variance budget is 44\% acoustic $+$ 43\% numeric versus only 2.5\% text (text
is L2-normalized, so its per-dimension scale is tiny), so full-matrix PCA-192 spends
\textbf{87\% of its variance budget} reconstructing the numeric $+$ acoustic blocks
to $R^2 = \num{1.000}$ while the signal-carrying text block reaches only $R^2 =
\num{0.717}$; per-column standardize (whitening) flips the captured budget to 73\%
text / 21\% categorical (text $R^2$ \num{0.905})
(Figure~\ref{fig:nexttrack-evr-budget}). Eight alternative item-latent spaces were
built parity-gated to the champion split (only \texttt{item\_latents.f32} differs)
and each evaluated under \emph{two} readouts: \textbf{(A)} the champion projection
\texttt{blend-gru-markov-content-proj} (the space's ceiling under the winning
recipe) and \textbf{(C)} the frozen \texttt{gru-infonce-h256} single (raw
representation quality). Validation-only; nothing promoted.

\begin{figure}[ht]
  \centering
  \includegraphics[width=0.78\textwidth]{nexttrack_evr_budget}
  \caption{Why the item vector is a handicap for a raw model --- and why the
  projection is the fix. The equal-weighted PCA-192 orders dimensions by raw
  variance, so 87\% of its captured budget reconstructs the behaviorally-inert
  numeric $+$ acoustic blocks (popularity / era / loudness) while the
  signal-carrying text block (2.5\% of raw input variance, L2-normalized) is
  under-preserved ($R^2$ \num{0.717}). Per-column whitening flips the captured
  budget to 73\% text / 21\% categorical (text $R^2$ \num{0.905}). The learned
  InfoNCE projection learns this same reweighting from data.}
  \label{fig:nexttrack-evr-budget}
\end{figure}

\begin{table}[ht]
  \centering
  \caption{Item-vector representation exploration: eight alternative 192-d item
  spaces vs the PCA-192 champion, under readout A (champion projection) and readout
  C (frozen GRU). Paired $\Delta$ on exact hit@10 (2000-resample bootstrap, rng
  1337) vs the same-readout baseline; a lift needs the CI entirely $>0$. Baseline
  rows and the best readout-C lift (V5) bolded. \emph{Not comparable to any rotation
  board.}}
  \label{tab:nexttrack-representation}
  {\scriptsize\setlength{\tabcolsep}{3pt}\begin{tabular}{llcccc}
    \toprule
    & & \multicolumn{2}{c}{Readout A (projection)} & \multicolumn{2}{c}{Readout C (frozen GRU)} \\
    \cmidrule(lr){3-4}\cmidrule(lr){5-6}
    Variant (space) & dataset id & R@10 & $\Delta$ hit@10 [CI] & R@10 & $\Delta$ hit@10 [CI] \\
    \midrule

    V1 noaco               & \dsid{seq-20260718-222742} & 0.2089 & $-0.0028$ [$-0.013,+0.008$] & 0.1481 & $+0.0252$ [$+0.009,+0.041$] \\
    V2 std (whiten all)    & \dsid{seq-20260718-222754} & 0.2082 & $-0.0035$ [$-0.015,+0.008$] & 0.1530 & $+0.0300$ [$+0.011,+0.049$] \\
    V3 std-noaco           & \dsid{seq-20260718-222807} & 0.2166 & $+0.0049$ [$-0.008,+0.017$] & 0.1572 & $+0.0342$ [$+0.015,+0.055$] \\
    V4 textcat             & \dsid{seq-20260718-222820} & 0.1950 & $-0.0168$ [$-0.029,-0.006$] & 0.1565 & $+0.0335$ [$+0.015,+0.051$] \\
    \best{V5 balanced-pca192} & \dsid{seq-20260718-222834} & 0.2159 & $+0.0042$ [$-0.006,+0.015$] & \best{0.1586} & \best{$+0.0356$ [$+0.019,+0.052$]} \\

    V10 text-only          & \dsid{seq-20260718-222901} & 0.1999 & $-0.0119$ [$-0.025,+0.001$] & 0.1377 & $+0.0147$ [$-0.005,+0.034$] \\
    V6 sonic-64 (64-d)     & \dsid{seq-20260718-222905} & 0.1936 & $-0.0182$ [$-0.031,-0.005$] & 0.1216 & $-0.0014$ [$-0.017,+0.013$] \\

  \end{tabular}}

\begin{figure}[ht]
  \centering
  \includegraphics[width=0.8\textwidth]{nexttrack_representation}
  \caption{The representation handicap is real for the \emph{raw} GRU but
  \emph{absorbed} by the champion projection. Six of eight whitening / acoustic-drop
  / metric spaces lift the frozen GRU (readout C, green; all six with paired-$\Delta$
  CI entirely $>0$), but none lifts the champion projection (readout A, blue; the two
  nominally-positive spaces straddle 0, and V4/V6 significantly regress). The learned
  InfoNCE map already re-weights away the loud acoustic / numeric directions:
  whitening and the projection are the same fix.}
  \label{fig:nexttrack-representation}
\end{figure}

\emph{Verdict.} \textbf{Readout A: no variant beats the PCA-192 champion with paired
$\Delta$ CI entirely $>0$} (V3 std-noaco $+\num{0.0049}$ and V5 balanced
$+\num{0.0042}$ straddle 0; V4 textcat $-\num{0.0168}$ and V6 sonic-64 $-\num{0.0182}$
regress). \textbf{Readout C: six of eight variants beat the frozen GRU with paired
$\Delta$ CI entirely $>0$} ($+\num{0.025}$ to $+\num{0.036}$). So the raw geometry
\emph{is} a handicap --- suppressing the loud directions lifts the raw GRU by
$\sim$20--29\% relative --- \textbf{but the gain is fully absorbed by the
projection}, which already re-weights those directions from data. Whitening and the
learned projection are the same fix, and the champion has already pulled it. The
graded axis moves more cleanly than exact recall (whitening lifts the frozen GRU's
genre@10 to $\sim$\num{0.47}; a text-only space posts the readout-A artist@10 /
genre@10 maxima), and the continuous music@10 metric
(Section~\ref{sec:nexttrack-music}) corroborates: the projection champion leads on
it (\num{0.4538}) over R+M+C (\num{0.4491}), and it is flat across the eight
representations ($\sim$\num{0.447}--\num{0.460}). One caution: dropping
numeric$+$acoustic \emph{entirely} (V4) hurts the champion even though it helps the
raw GRU, so keep numeric and whiten / drop only acoustic.
\emph{Source: \texttt{2026-07-18c-nexttrack-representation-exploration.md}.}

\section{Next-track: the music@10 musical-distance metric}
\label{sec:nexttrack-music}
\emph{Rationale.} Exact Recall@10 is unforgiving on a 55\%-cold single-user corpus.
This campaign introduces a \emph{continuous} ``sounds-alike'' graded metric,
\textbf{music@10}, and back-fills it across every next-track run. It is the mean
over test cases of the best (max) cosine, in a \emph{balanced} intrinsic
musical-distance space (\dsid{spotify\_tracks\_content\_metric}: a block-weighted,
partial-whitened fusion of the whitened 64-d song-AE latent, the 384-d name/tag text
embedding, five metadata numerics, and a per-genre AE prototype), between the true
next track and the top-ten retrieved candidates. A top-ten containing the exact
truth scores \num{1.0}, so music@10 is a \emph{smooth superset} of Recall@10; it is
display-only (the primary metric stays Recall@10).

\emph{Results.} music@10 was recomputed offline and non-destructively across all 53
next-track runs (from each run's stored \texttt{predictions.json} and its dataset
\texttt{items.json}, on the shared \num{1431}-case split). It \textbf{correlates}
with Recall@10 ($r = \num{0.609}$) but is far from redundant, and it crowns a
\emph{different} winner (Table~\ref{tab:nexttrack-music-families};
Figure~\ref{fig:nexttrack-music-scatter}). The Recall@10 champion is a
\texttt{seq-blend} run (\num{0.223}), but the \emph{musically-closest} predictions
come from \texttt{seq-nexttrack} --- which regresses the next track's content latent
directly and retrieves by cosine --- at best music@10 \num{0.482} and the highest
family mean (\num{0.448}). The \texttt{seq-popularity} floor is correctly worst
(\num{0.185}), confirming the metric is not trivially saturated.

\begin{table}[ht]
  \centering
  \caption{music@10 by predictor family, from the offline backfill over all 53
  next-track runs (one shared \num{1431}-case split). The recall@10 champion
  (\texttt{seq-blend}) is \emph{not} the music@10 champion (\texttt{seq-nexttrack}).
  Best cell per column bolded.}
  \label{tab:nexttrack-music-families}
  {\footnotesize\setlength{\tabcolsep}{6pt}\begin{tabular}{lcccc}
    \toprule
    Predictor family & runs & best recall@10 & best music@10 & mean music@10 \\
    \midrule
    seq-blend      & 23 & \best{0.223} & 0.461 & 0.441 \\
    seq-nexttrack  & 24 & 0.159 & \best{0.482} & \best{0.448} \\
    seq-markov     & 1  & 0.107 & 0.407 & 0.407 \\
    seq-ann        & 1  & 0.090 & 0.426 & 0.426 \\
    seq-stacker    & 3  & 0.087 & 0.418 & 0.411 \\
    seq-popularity & 1  & 0.001 & 0.185 & 0.185 \\
    \bottomrule
  \end{tabular}}
\end{table}

\begin{figure}[ht]
  \centering
  \includegraphics[width=0.82\textwidth]{nexttrack_music_scatter}
  \caption{music@10 versus Recall@10 across all 53 next-track runs, one point per
  run, coloured by predictor family. The two axes correlate ($r = \num{0.609}$) but
  crown different winners --- the Recall@10 champion (a \texttt{seq-blend} run at
  \num{0.223}) is not the musically-closest, which is a \texttt{seq-nexttrack} run
  (music@10 \num{0.482}) that regresses the content latent and retrieves by cosine.
  music@10 is display-only; Recall@10 stays the primary metric.}
  \label{fig:nexttrack-music-scatter}
\end{figure}

\emph{Interpretation.} music@10 is a genuinely distinct, non-saturated view of
next-track quality: it rewards \emph{sonic} proximity, which the exact-hit metric
ignores. The recall-versus-music decoupling has a clean mechanism --- a model that
regresses the content latent (\texttt{seq-nexttrack}) lands \emph{near} the truth in
content space even when it misses the exact next track, so its misses ``sound
right'' more often than the blend's. This is why the metric is display-only and must
not silently become a selection objective; promotion still ranks by Recall@10. It
inherits a mild circularity from the \emph{balanced} dial (the text block embeds the
artist name; the genre block is a genre prototype), so it partly rewards
same-artist / genre proximity.
\emph{Source: \texttt{2026-07-20-musical-distance-metric.md}.}

\section{Next-track: session holisticness metrics and the anti-eager levers (MMR $\lambda$ / eager $\beta$)}
\label{sec:nexttrack-holisticness}
\emph{Rationale.} A recurring qualitative complaint about the top blends is that
their predictions feel ``too album-eager / bigram-like'' --- they parrot the
current artist rather than curating a holistic continuation. This campaign
quantifies that symptom with a newly-added \emph{display-only session-holisticness}
metric suite, tests the user's hypothesis that \emph{the GRU beats the LSTM because
it is more eager}, and measures whether two new Phase-2 levers reduce eagerness at
an acceptable recall cost: an eval-time MMR re-rank (\texttt{mmr\_lambda}) and an
anti-eager training regularizer (\texttt{eager\_beta}). It is a measurement /
exploratory pass: nothing is promoted, the primary metric stays Recall@10, and the
holisticness objective decision is explicitly deferred to the user. Every arm runs
on the \emph{canonical} PCA-192 champion split \dsid{seq-20260715-131139}
(\num{1431} test sessions; cold-item rate \num{0.55}), and the champion reproduced
its on-record Recall@10 \num{0.2117} exactly (\num{0.21174}).

\paragraph{The new display-only holisticness metrics.} Wired end-to-end alongside
music@10 (\texttt{seq\_common.eval\_from\_scores} $\to$ Rust $\to$
\texttt{domain.toml} $\to$ UI); the primary metric is unchanged.
\begin{description}
  \item[artist\_adj@10 ($\downarrow$).] Fraction of the top-10 sharing the
  \emph{seed} track's artist --- the ``parrots the current artist'' eagerness rate.
  \textbf{Lower is better}.
  \item[album\_adj@10.] The same on (artist, album); \textbf{omitted on this
  dataset} because its \texttt{items.json} carries no album field (expected, not an
  error --- covered by a separate album-carrying dataset).
  \item[mood\_coh@10 ($\uparrow$).] Mean cosine of the top-10 to the prefix mood
  centroid in the content-metric space; higher is more mood-coherent.
  \item[ild@10 ($\uparrow$).] Intra-list diversity (mean pairwise sonic distance) of
  the top-10; higher is less duplicative.
  \item[suffix\_recall@10 / cont\_prec@10.] Multi-step continuation coverage,
  produced only by the on-demand harness \texttt{seq\_continuation\_eval.py}
  (Phase E below), not by normal runs.
\end{description}
The sanity gate passed: music@10 / mood\_coh@10 / ild@10 populate on all blend and
GRU runs, so the content-metric vectors loaded and the MMR / mood / ild levers are
live, not no-ops. Both new levers are OFF by default and verified byte-identical
when off.

\emph{Results --- the holisticness fingerprint (Phase A).} Table~\ref{tab:nexttrack-holisticness-fingerprint}
fingerprints the model family. \textbf{Eagerness tracks Recall@10 inside the blend
family} --- the champion (Recall@10 \num{0.2117}) is the \emph{most} artist-eager
model measured (artist\_adj@10 \num{0.610}), with R+M next (\num{0.579}) --- but the
eagerness lives in the \emph{Markov} leg, not the RNN: the standalone first-order
Markov bar is already at \num{0.543}, while the single GRU / LSTM sit far lower at
artist\_adj@10 $\approx$ \num{0.345}. So the blends buy their Recall@10 lead by being
artist-adjacent (bigram-like), which is exactly what the holisticness suite flags.
The single RNNs are conversely the \emph{mood-coherent, low-eager} models (highest
mood\_coh@10, lowest real-model artist\_adj@10). Crucially the metrics are \emph{not
trivially gamed}: the popularity floor maxes ild@10 (\num{0.898}) and minimizes
artist\_adj@10 (\num{0.003}) yet scores $\approx 0$ recall and \emph{negative}
mood\_coh@10 --- you maximize them by being random, so the meaningful low-eager /
diverse models are the real single RNNs.

\begin{table}[ht]
  \centering
  \caption{Phase A holisticness fingerprint across the model family, sorted by
  Recall@10 (\num{1431} test sessions). All runs share the prefix
  \protect\runid{run-20260722-165345-}; the id column lists the distinguishing suffix.
  Recall@10 (raw hits of \num{1431}), MRR, artist@10, genre@10, music@10,
  mood\_coh@10, ild@10 higher-better; \textbf{artist\_adj@10 lower-better}. Champion
  row and best cell per metric bolded. \textsuperscript{\dag}~The popularity floor's
  extreme artist\_adj@10 / ild@10 are \emph{degenerate} (it ignores the seed): it
  lands $\approx 0$ recall and negative mood\_coh@10, proving the metrics are not
  gamed low/high by a good model.}
  \label{tab:nexttrack-holisticness-fingerprint}
  {\scriptsize\setlength{\tabcolsep}{3pt}\begin{tabular}{llcccccccc}
    \toprule
    Config & id & R@10 (raw) & MRR & art@10 & gen@10 & mus@10 & artadj@10\,$\downarrow$ & mood@10\,$\uparrow$ & ild@10\,$\uparrow$ \\
    \midrule
    \best{A1 champion \Rp+M+\Cp} & \texttt{82a8f} & \best{0.2117 (303)} & \best{0.122} & 0.333 & 0.401 & 0.454 & 0.610 & 0.398 & 0.406 \\
    A2 R+M (blend)               & \texttt{28dbb} & 0.1712 (245) & 0.095 & 0.329 & 0.398 & 0.443 & 0.579 & 0.399 & 0.403 \\
    A3 GRU alone ($\beta{=}0$)   & \texttt{e39c9} & 0.1230 (176) & 0.053 & \best{0.338} & 0.450 & \best{0.456} & 0.347 & \best{0.479} & 0.454 \\
    B LSTM (else matched to A3)  & \texttt{bb387} & 0.1188 (170) & 0.049 & 0.328 & \best{0.455} & 0.449 & 0.344 & 0.458 & 0.463 \\
    A4 markov (first-order)      & \texttt{047cc} & 0.1069 (153) & 0.069 & 0.298 & 0.389 & 0.407 & 0.543 & 0.310 & 0.462 \\
    A5 popularity                & \texttt{34614} & 0.0007 (1)   & 0.001 & 0.014 & 0.085 & 0.185 & \best{0.003}\textsuperscript{\dag} & $-0.041$ & \best{0.898}\textsuperscript{\dag} \\
    \bottomrule
  \end{tabular}}
\end{table}

\emph{GRU versus LSTM (Phase B) --- the hypothesis is not supported.} With
hyperparameters matched (h256, InfoNCE, 40 epochs, lr \num{1e-3}), the GRU and LSTM
are statistically \emph{tied}: Recall@10 \num{0.1230} versus \num{0.1188} ($\Delta$
$+\num{0.0042}$, inside the $\pm\num{0.015}$ band), and artist\_adj@10 \num{0.347}
versus \num{0.344} (a negligible $+\num{0.003}$). The GRU is not measurably more
eager than the LSTM on this split, so the ``GRU wins because it parrots the current
artist harder'' hypothesis is \textbf{refuted here}.

\emph{The MMR $\lambda$ lever (Phase C) --- the strong knob.} MMR is a pure
eval-time re-rank, so the four originally-launched per-$\lambda$ training runs were
\emph{cancelled} (not failed experiments); the sweep was computed offline from the
single champion checkpoint (the $\lambda{=}$off cell reproduced the champion's
Recall@10 \num{0.2117} exactly). As $\lambda$ drops from off toward \num{0.3}
(Table~\ref{tab:nexttrack-mmr-sweep}; Figure~\ref{fig:nexttrack-mmr-tradeoff}), Recall@10 falls
--- the \emph{only} quantity traded --- while ild@10 rises and artist\_adj@10 falls
(de-eagering). \textbf{$\lambda{=}\num{0.9}$ is essentially free}: the recall
$\Delta$ is $-\num{0.0014}$, well inside the $\pm\num{0.015}$ band (a tie), yet it
already lifts ild@10 \num{0.406}$\to$\num{0.436} and trims eagerness
\num{0.610}$\to$\num{0.603}. \textbf{$\lambda\approx\num{0.7}$} trades $\sim$2 recall
points ($\Delta$ $-\num{0.0181}$, just outside the band) for a large ild@10 gain
(\num{0.406}$\to$\num{0.520}) and a real eagerness drop
(\num{0.610}$\to$\num{0.571}). A notable side effect: \textbf{music@10 rises
monotonically} as you diversify (\num{0.454}$\to$\num{0.492}) --- sonic closeness and
list diversity are not in tension; only exact recall is traded.

  \textbf{artist\_adj@10 lower-better}. Best cell per metric bolded; the champion
  default ($\lambda{=}$off) recall / MRR bolded.}

    \midrule

    0.7 & 0.1936 (277) & $-0.0181$ & 0.116 & 0.347 & 0.476 & 0.571 & 0.364 & 0.520 \\
    0.5 & 0.1642 (235) & $-0.0475$ & 0.108 & \best{0.359} & 0.487 & 0.486 & 0.315 & 0.653 \\
    0.3 & 0.1097 (157) & $-0.1020$ & 0.100 & 0.358 & \best{0.492} & \best{0.293} & 0.238 & \best{0.815} \\

  \end{tabular}}
\end{table}

  traded --- while ild@10 rises and artist\_adj@10 falls (right axis; more diverse,
  less artist-eager) and music@10 even \emph{rises}. $\lambda{=}\num{0.9}$ sits inside
  the $\pm\num{0.015}$ recall tie band yet already lifts ild@10 by \num{0.030};

  \label{fig:nexttrack-mmr-tradeoff}

\emph{The anti-eager regularizer $\beta$ (Phase D) --- the weak knob.} A
training-time penalty on near-copy prediction of the current track's latent
(\texttt{eager\_margin} \num{0.0}) barely moves the needle
(Table~\ref{tab:nexttrack-eager-beta}): across $\beta$ \numrange{0}{0.2}, artist\_adj@10 shifts
only \num{0.347}$\to$\num{0.340} ($\Delta$ $-\num{0.0074}$) and ild@10 only
\num{0.454}$\to$\num{0.458}, with Recall@10 flat (all four inside $\pm\num{0.015}$).
The single GRU is already low-eager (\num{0.347}), so there is little copy behaviour
left to penalize --- $\beta$ is not a useful holisticness knob at these settings.

\emph{Multi-step continuation (Phase E).} The on-demand harness
(\texttt{seq\_continuation\_eval.py} \texttt{-m 3 -k 10}, \num{1172} continuation
cases) shows the GRU \emph{beats} the Markov bigram on both multi-step metrics
(Table~\ref{tab:nexttrack-continuation}): the RNN's advantage \emph{widens} past the immediate
next track, because the bigram's single-step co-occurrence does not compound into
good 3-step continuations whereas the GRU's learned sequence state does --- the
mirror image of the single-step board, where the bigram-carrying blend wins raw
Recall@10.

\begin{table}[ht]
  \centering
  \caption{Phase E multi-step continuation (harness \texttt{-m 3 -k 10},
  \num{1172} cases) on \dsid{seq-20260715-131139}: the GRU (A3 checkpoint) beats the
  first-order Markov bigram on both metrics (higher-better). Winner bolded.}
  \label{tab:nexttrack-continuation}
  {\footnotesize\setlength{\tabcolsep}{6pt}\begin{tabular}{lcc}
    \toprule
    Model & suffix\_recall@10 & cont\_prec@10 \\
    \midrule
    markov (first-order bigram) & 0.0869 & 0.0236 \\
    \best{GRU (gru-infonce-h256, A3)} & \best{0.0963} & \best{0.0273} \\
    \bottomrule
  \end{tabular}}
\end{table}

\emph{Interpretation and the deferred decision.} The campaign delivers a clean
mechanistic account of the eagerness symptom: it is sourced from the Markov bigram
leg (which parrots the current artist via raw co-occurrence), not the neural leg, so
any future de-eagering should target the bigram / the fusion, not the RNN. Of the two
new levers, the eval-time MMR re-rank is the \emph{strong} knob and the training-time
$\beta$ regularizer is \emph{weak}. Nothing is promoted --- the primary metric stays
Recall@10 and the champion is unchanged --- but \textbf{$\lambda\approx\num{0.9}$ is
flagged as a near-free holisticness upgrade for a future user promotion decision}
(Recall@10 tie within noise, ild@10 \num{0.406}$\to$\num{0.436}, eagerness
\num{0.610}$\to$\num{0.603}), with $\lambda\approx\num{0.7}$ the ``meaningful
de-eagering for $\sim$2 recall points'' operating point; adopting either requires a
user decision plus a 3-seed / native-run confirm. The $\beta$ regularizer is not
recommended as a lever at these settings.
\emph{Source: \texttt{2026-07-22-session-holisticness-and-antieager-levers.md}.}

\section{Next-track: dual-tower fusion refuted --- the fourth combiner null}
\label{sec:nexttrack-dualtower}
\emph{Rationale.} By 2026-07-25 the record held three independent
\emph{learned-combiner} nulls --- the XGB stacker (\numrange{0.080}{0.087},
\emph{below} its own GRU base leg; Section~\ref{sec:nexttrack-modelsweep}), reciprocal-rank
fusion ($-\num{0.029}$) and even \emph{oracle} test-fit blend weights
($+\num{0.004}$, CI straddling zero; Section~\ref{sec:nexttrack-projection-win}). All three
were \textbf{post-hoc, score-level} combiners, which left one obvious rebuttal
standing: \emph{of course} a bolted-on combiner fails --- train the fusion
end-to-end so the parts co-adapt. This campaign runs exactly that rebuttal, at
the \emph{representation} level, jointly, from scratch.

The vehicle is a new predictor, \texttt{seq-dualgru}: two \emph{independent}
recurrent towers (separate weights) read the prefix in parallel over
configurable \emph{causal views}, their per-step hidden states are concatenated,
and one MLP head fuses them into the predicted next-item latent. Three views are
exposed, all strictly causal at step $t$: \texttt{latent} (the raw item vector),
\texttt{delta} (the step-to-step difference, $x_{-1}=0$), and \texttt{cummean}
(the running session centroid --- the quantity the \texttt{seq-ann} pooled-MLP
leg was built from). Crucially it reuses \texttt{seq\_nexttrack.make\_batches}
and \texttt{step\_loss} verbatim, so objective, batching, early stopping and
evaluation are byte-for-byte the family's, and it carries no bidirectional mode
(a backward pass would leak the teacher-forcing target).

The hypothesis was sharp: two towers should beat one \emph{only if they see
genuinely different views}; two towers on the same \texttt{latent} view should
reduce to a capacity / implicit-ensemble effect a wider single GRU can buy more
cheaply. The design therefore crosses the \emph{complete upper triangle} of the
$\{\texttt{latent},\texttt{delta},\texttt{cummean}\}^2$ view matrix (six cells at
\texttt{fusion\_layers}${}=1$) with a \emph{fusion-depth} axis (two cells at
\texttt{fusion\_layers}${}=0$, a pure linear read of the concatenated states) and
--- the axis the earlier topology scan lacked (Section~\ref{sec:nexttrack-topology}) --- a
\textbf{parameter-matched single-GRU capacity control}. Parameter counts were
re-measured by instantiating the real classes at \texttt{latent\_dim}${}=192$:
the control C1 (h425, \num{871017} parameters) matches the six
\texttt{fusion\_layers}${}=1$ dual arms (\num{871872}) to
\SI{0.098}{\percent}, and is \SI{10}{\percent} \emph{larger} than the
\texttt{fusion\_layers}${}=0$ arms (\num{789696}) --- deliberately generous to
the single tower.

\paragraph{The honest bar.} Two boards were pre-registered \emph{separately}, so
that the scientific verdict and the board event could not be traded off after the
fact. On \textbf{Recall@10} the bar for a standalone single model is the
single-model board \num{0.123}, \emph{not} the three-leg blend champion
\num{0.212} --- comparing one trained model against a fusion of three legs would
be a category error, and dual-tower-as-the-\Rp-leg is a \texttt{seq-blend} code
change explicitly out of scope. On \textbf{holisticness@10} the crown rule
carries an anti-degenerate recall floor (see below). All ten runs are on the
canonical split \dsid{seq-20260715-131139} at seed 1337, executed one at a time
on a single remote worker, and the baseline C0 reproduced its on-record
Recall@10 \num{0.12299} (\num{176} of \num{1431}) \emph{exactly}.

\emph{Results.} Table~\ref{tab:nexttrack-dualtower} is the batch. \textbf{No arm is bolded
as a winner because there is no winner}: the single-GRU baseline holds the
batch's best Recall@10, and not one of the eight dual arms beats it by
paired-$\Delta$ CI above zero --- six lose outright and two (the
\texttt{fusion\_layers}${}=0$ pair) tie. The pre-registered refutation rule
fired.

\emph{Capacity is not the missed lever either.} The param-matched control C1
(h425, \num{871017} parameters --- $2.2\times$ C0) scores \num{0.12089} against
C0's \num{0.12299}, a paired $\Delta$ of $-\num{0.0021}$ straddling zero. This
fills a real gap: single-GRU \emph{width} had only ever been scanned on the
64-dim AE space (Section~\ref{sec:nexttrack-topology}), and the rich PCA-192 space was
untested. It behaves the same way, so \textbf{single-GRU width is now closed on
PCA-192 as well as AE-64}. Because C1 is a genuine \SI{0.098}{\percent}
parameter match to the \texttt{fusion\_layers}${}=1$ arms, the null cannot be
re-attributed to ``capacity would have helped'': it demonstrably would not.

\emph{View attribution --- complementarity is real, but lands on the wrong
pair.} The complete view triangle is what makes this mechanically decidable
rather than a judgment call, and the answer is genuinely surprising
(Table~\ref{tab:nexttrack-dualtower-views}, Figure~\ref{fig:nexttrack-dualtower-matrix}).
The pair the design nominated as its best hope --- \texttt{latent} $+$
\texttt{cummean} --- is \emph{not} complementary: it ties its latent/latent
parent and beats only the centroid-only one, i.e.\ the raw-latent tower carries
the cell and the centroid tower is dead weight. Same for \texttt{latent} $+$
\texttt{delta}. But \texttt{delta} $+$ \texttt{cummean} \emph{is} complementary
--- it beats \emph{both} parents with CI above zero. Read together: the
co-adaptation mechanism the hypothesis proposed genuinely \textbf{works}; it is
simply that \textbf{the raw \texttt{latent} view already subsumes everything the
other two carry}. A GRU over raw latents integrates step-to-step movement and
the running mean internally, so pairing it with an explicit computation of
either is redundant --- while two \emph{impoverished} views do have
non-overlapping information to pool, at a hopeless absolute level
(\num{0.08665}). That is a clean mechanistic explanation for the null, not
merely an absence of effect.

  \centering

  single-view parents (paired $\Delta$ Recall@10, \num{1431} sessions). Only one
  of three mixed pairs is genuinely complementary --- and it is the one with no
  raw-latent tower.}
  \label{tab:nexttrack-dualtower-views}
  {\scriptsize\setlength{\tabcolsep}{3.4pt}\begin{tabular}{llll}
    \toprule
    Mixed cell & vs parent 1 & vs parent 2 & Verdict \\
    \midrule
    D-LC \emph{lat/cum} & D-LL: $-0.0056$ [$-.018$, $+.007$] & D-CC: $+0.0308$ [$+.018$, $+.045$] & \emph{not} complementary \\
    D-LD \emph{lat/del} & D-LL: $-0.0112$ [$-.023$, $+.001$] & D-DD: $+0.0335$ [$+.020$, $+.047$] & \emph{not} complementary \\
    \best{D-DC \emph{del/cum}} & D-DD: $+0.0287$ [$+.015$, $+.043$] & D-CC: $+0.0203$ [$+.008$, $+.034$] & \best{complementary: both CI$>$0} \\
    \bottomrule
  \end{tabular}}
\end{table}

\begin{figure}[ht]
  \centering
  \includegraphics[width=0.8\textwidth]{nexttrack_dualtower_matrix}
  \caption{The complete view-pairing matrix at \texttt{fusion\_layers}${}=1$,
  one run per cell. Every dual cell sits \emph{below} the single-GRU baseline
  (\num{0.12299}), and the ordering is monotone in how much raw \texttt{latent}
  the pair contains: both-latent is best, both-delta worst. The lower triangle
  was never run (the pairing is symmetric by construction) and is masked in grey
  rather than mirrored, so the colour scale reflects only measured cells. The
  gradient is the finding: a recurrent tower over raw latents already computes
  what \texttt{delta} and \texttt{cummean} make explicit.}
  \label{fig:nexttrack-dualtower-matrix}
\end{figure}

\emph{The MLP fusion head is actively harmful, not merely useless.} Both
\texttt{fusion\_layers}${}=0$ arms beat their \texttt{fusion\_layers}${}=1$ twins
by paired-$\Delta$ CI above zero ($+\num{0.0161}$ on latent/latent,
$+\num{0.0203}$ on latent/cummean) at \SI{10}{\percent} \emph{fewer} parameters
--- so removing the head makes the model measurably better in \emph{both} view
regimes. Mechanistically this fits the whole family arc: the objective is
cosine/InfoNCE retrieval \emph{in} the item-latent space, so the head's job is
to land \emph{in} that geometry, and an extra 256-wide non-linear layer between
the recurrent state and a metric read-out just adds a warp the loss must then
undo. The trade-off runs the other way on holisticness (both f0 arms have
\emph{lower} H, $\Delta$H CI below zero): the f1 arms' extra warp diffuses the
top-ten (higher ild, lower artist\_adj) at the cost of exact hits. The rule
adopted is \textbf{default \texttt{fusion\_layers}${}=0$}, later confirmed a
third time (Table~\ref{tab:nexttrack-fusion-depth}).

\emph{Holisticness and recall are anti-correlated, and the recall floor earned
its place.} Five arms lift holisticness@10 by paired-$\Delta$H CI above zero,
topping out at D-CC's \num{0.19715} ($+\num{0.055}$) --- which on the naked crown
metric would have ranked second on the entire board. But D-CC lands \num{95} of
\num{1431} exact hits against the baseline's \num{176}. The ordering of the batch
by H is almost the \emph{reverse} of its ordering by recall
(Figure~\ref{fig:nexttrack-holisticness-antiwin}), so \textbf{all five
$\Delta$H winners fail the pre-registered $\pm\num{0.015}$ recall floor} and the
crown rule never fired. Without that floor the campaign would have ``won the
crown'' with a model \SI{46}{\percent} worse at its actual job --- precisely the
degenerate \texttt{mood-session} failure mode (H \num{0.2332} at Recall@10
\num{0.0238}) the floor was written to block. On the present evidence,
\textbf{unconstrained holisticness is maximized by predicting less well}, so any
future holisticness objective on this corpus must be a \emph{constrained} one.

\begin{figure}[ht]
  \centering
  \includegraphics[width=0.8\textwidth]{nexttrack_holisticness_antiwin}
  \caption{Why the crown rule needs a hard recall floor. Across the ten arms,
  holisticness@10 runs almost exactly \emph{backwards} along Recall@10: the five
  arms that clear the $\Delta$H CI-above-zero leg (green) are the five furthest
  \emph{below} the baseline on exact recall, and all five fall outside the
  $\pm\num{0.015}$ floor (shaded). The open square is the incumbent
  \texttt{mood-session} model that motivated the floor --- the highest
  holisticness on record at \num{34} of \num{1431} correct next tracks. H values
  are the 3-factor generation (Section~\ref{sec:nexttrack-power}).}
  \label{fig:nexttrack-holisticness-antiwin}
\end{figure}

\emph{Interpretation.} Nothing was registered and nothing promoted; the champion,
the leaderboard and the pins are untouched. What the campaign buys is a
\emph{closure}: the combiner null is now robust across the whole combiner design
space on this corpus --- post-hoc score-level (three ways) \emph{and}
representation-level jointly-trained with a parameter-matched control --- which
materially consolidates ``the fixed equal-thirds z-blend is the combiner of
record.'' Two genuine sub-findings survive the null (the harmful MLP fusion head,
and \texttt{delta}$+$\texttt{cummean} complementarity), and the machinery itself
is sound: 10 of 10 runs succeeded, the new predictor trains, converges and
evaluates at exact parity with the family, and D-LL-f0 even posts the batch's
best MRR (\num{0.05631} against C0's \num{0.05341}) --- the one metric on which a
dual arm leads. Per the noise-band discipline that is ``at least equal, likely
neither better nor worse,'' not a win.

\paragraph{A provenance finding that revises two earlier numbers.} Before the
batch, the live remote worker directory was found \emph{stale}: its
\texttt{seq\_common.py} had no \texttt{holisticness\_at\_k} at all,
\texttt{seq\_nexttrack.py} differed from the hub by md5, and three
\texttt{seq\_*.py} modules were missing entirely. After refreshing and verifying
the whole family md5-identical to the hub, C0 returned \num{0.12299},
bit-identical to the hub's on-record value --- \emph{not} the \num{0.1201957}
that two earlier remote runs (\runid{run-20260723-002044-af11a},
\texttt{-17cf8}) had returned. The ``cross-host reduction-order offset of
$-\num{0.0028}$'' those runs had suggested is therefore \textbf{withdrawn: it was
a code difference, not a host effect}. Their recall numbers belong to a
superseded code state and are not code-comparable to later batches; they remain
valid as the $\beta$-eagerness measurements they were taken for
(Table~\ref{tab:nexttrack-eager-beta}), since those conclusions rest on within-batch
comparisons. The general lesson --- a divergent remote checkout produces a
\emph{wrong number}, not a crash --- is now a standing pre-flight gate.
\emph{Source: \texttt{2026-07-25-nexttrack-dual-tower-fusion-scan.md}.}

\section{Next-track: the per-step pre-encoder refuted, and the rectifier mechanism}
\label{sec:nexttrack-preencoder}
\emph{Rationale.} The dual-tower null closed \emph{deterministic} view
asymmetry: \texttt{delta} and \texttt{cummean} are lossy functions of what a
raw-latent tower already holds, so pairing them buys nothing. This campaign asks
the strictly stronger version --- does a \textbf{learned} per-step
re-embedding, which is \emph{not} a function of information the tower already
has, enlarge the hypothesis space usefully? The axis is a nonlinear
re-embedding in front of the recurrence,
$[\,\text{Linear}(d_{\text{in}},w),\ \text{ReLU},\ \text{Dropout}\,]\times
\texttt{pre\_layers}$, applied per timestep (strictly causal), with the GRU's
\texttt{input\_size} becoming $w$.

The hypothesis has an unusually sharp analytic edge. A GRU's own input transform
is \emph{already linear} ($W_i x$ per gate), and a \emph{linear} pre-encoder at
$w \ge d_{\text{in}}$ is exactly as expressive as a bare GRU, since
$W_i(Vx) = (W_iV)x$ with no rank constraint. \textbf{The ReLU is therefore the
only expressivity change}, which means no linear-pre control run is needed and a
null cannot be explained away as ``the pre-encoder was redundant by
construction.'' The design's decisive move is the \textbf{single-tower ablation}
S1 --- a \texttt{MLP256}$\to$\texttt{GRU256} against a parameter-matched plain
GRU --- because without it any dual-tower result is uninterpretable: pre-encoder,
or second tower?

Ten runs, same canonical split, same single remote worker, zero failures. Two
reproduction gates both passed \emph{bit-identically}: C0 returned Recall@10
\num{0.12299091544374564} (\num{176} of \num{1431}, identical to the last digit,
a seventh reproduction), confirming the \texttt{pre\_hidden} edit did not perturb
the family's default code path; and C3, the bare dual-tower arm, returned
\num{0.10272536687631029}, exactly its on-record value, validating every dual
verdict.

\emph{Results.} Table~\ref{tab:nexttrack-preencoder} is the batch and
Figure~\ref{fig:nexttrack-preencoder-forest} is the verdict at a glance. Not one
of the six pre-MLP arms beats the baseline by paired-$\Delta$ CI above zero:
five lose outright, one ties. \textbf{And the decisive ablation does not merely
tie --- it loses}: S1 at \num{0.10901} against C0's \num{0.12299}, $\Delta$
$-\num{0.0140}$ with the CI entirely below zero, \num{156} hits against
\num{176}. Since a linear pre-encoder here is provably expressivity-neutral,
\textbf{the ReLU re-embedding is destroying prefix information that the GRU's own
gated linear input transform preserved}.

\emph{The dose curve is flat, and placement is irrelevant.} Sweeping
\texttt{pre\_hidden\_b} through $\{0,128,256,384\}$ at
\texttt{fusion\_layers}${}=1$ (Table~\ref{tab:nexttrack-preencoder-dose}), every adjacent
pair straddles zero and so do the endpoints --- a \textbf{flat} axis, which by
the pre-registered clause would have blocked registration even had a win rule
fired. But note the \emph{nominal} ordering is downward-then-flat, never upward:
every dosed arm sits below the bare dual. Taken with S1's significant loss, the
sign of this axis is consistently negative across both towers and all four
widths, so there is no interior peak to chase. Symmetry is likewise a
non-factor: T1 (asymmetric, one bare tower) against T4 (both towers rectified)
straddles zero on recall, so keeping tower A bare is not load-bearing at this
dose.

\begin{table}[ht]
  \centering
  \caption{The dose curve, as measured: paired $\Delta$ Recall@10 against the
  bare dual-tower arm C3 at \texttt{fusion\_layers}${}=1$. Flat by the
  pre-registered test --- but nominally downward, never upward.}
  \label{tab:nexttrack-preencoder-dose}
  {\footnotesize\setlength{\tabcolsep}{6pt}\begin{tabular}{rlll}
    \toprule
    \texttt{pre\_hidden\_b} & Arm & R@10 (raw) & $\Delta$ vs C3 (bare) \\
    \midrule
    0 & C3 & 0.10273 (147) & --- \\
    128 & T2 & 0.09993 (143) & $-0.0028$ [$-0.0147$, $+0.0091$] straddles \\
    256 & T1 & 0.09713 (139) & $-0.0056$ [$-0.0189$, $+0.0077$] straddles \\
    384 & T3 & 0.09993 (143) & $-0.0028$ [$-0.0154$, $+0.0105$] straddles \\
    \bottomrule
  \end{tabular}}
\end{table}

\emph{The one solid positive: the fusion-depth regression, measured a third
time and bigger.} T1-f0 beats T1 by $+\num{0.0266}$ with the CI above zero,
against the $+\num{0.016}$ and $+\num{0.020}$ measured in the two dual-tower view
regimes (Table~\ref{tab:nexttrack-fusion-depth}). The mechanism holds and sharpens: every
extra nonlinear layer between the recurrent state and the metric read-out adds a
warp the InfoNCE loss must undo, and \emph{the more nonlinearity already added on
the input side, the more the output-side warp costs}. Note too that T1-f0 --- the
only pre arm that ties the baseline --- is exactly the arm with \emph{both} a bare
tower A \emph{and} \texttt{fusion\_layers}${}=0$: the shortest un-warped path from
raw latents to the read-out. \texttt{fusion\_layers}${}=0$ is the family default,
full stop.

\begin{table}[ht]
  \centering
  \caption{The \texttt{fusion\_layers} regression, three independent
  measurements. Paired $\Delta$ Recall@10 of the linear-read arm minus its
  MLP-head twin; positive means removing the fusion MLP \emph{helps}. The
  $\Delta$H column is available only for the two same-batch 3-factor pairs.
  \emph{Sources: \texttt{2026-07-25-nexttrack-dual-tower-fusion-scan.md} (rows
  1--2), \texttt{2026-07-26-nexttrack-pre-encoder-scan.md} (row 3).}}
  \label{tab:nexttrack-fusion-depth}
  {\scriptsize\setlength{\tabcolsep}{4pt}\begin{tabular}{llll}
    \toprule
    Pair (f0 $-$ f1) & Regime & $\Delta$ R@10 [95\% CI] & $\Delta$H [95\% CI] \\
    \midrule
    D-LL-f0 $-$ D-LL & redundant views, no pre-encoder & $+0.0161$ [$+0.0021$, $+0.0301$] & $-0.0086$ [$-0.0116$, $-0.0055$] \\
    D-LC-f0 $-$ D-LC & complementary views, no pre-encoder & $+0.0203$ [$+0.0063$, $+0.0335$] & $-0.0135$ [$-0.0171$, $-0.0100$] \\
    \best{T1-f0 $-$ T1} & \best{with a per-step pre-encoder} & \best{$+0.0266$ [$+0.0140$, $+0.0391$]} & --- \\
    \bottomrule
  \end{tabular}}
\end{table}

\emph{The mechanism, measured rather than inferred.} The analytic argument named
the rectifier as the only candidate; a follow-up interpretability read then
\emph{measured} it (Figure~\ref{fig:nexttrack-rectifier}). The decisive control
was a counterfactual tap the sparse-autoencoder engine cannot express --- the
model's \emph{own} affine map with the ReLU deleted. Reading next-item genre
decodability at the same step (\num{11184} probe test rows, 25 classes, paired
standard error $\le\num{0.0065}$): the raw latent scores \num{0.4033}, the
affine map \num{0.4084} (\textbf{neutral, ${\sim}0.8\sigma$}, exactly as
$W_i(Vx)=(W_iV)x$ predicts), and the rectified tap \num{0.3700}
(\textbf{${\sim}5.9\sigma$ --- the ReLU is the whole loss}). The smoking gun is
arithmetic: \texttt{frac\_exact\_zero} on the rectified tap is \num{0.5327},
matching the affine tap's \texttt{frac\_negative\_coords} \num{0.5327} to four
digits. The rectifier deletes \emph{precisely} the negative half, leaving
${\sim}120$ live units for 192 signed directions --- \emph{fewer live coordinates
than the input has dimensions}. PCA-192 latents are zero-centred and
near-symmetric by construction, and at $w=256$ (only $1.33\times$ the input
dimension) there is nowhere near enough over-completeness to re-encode the
discarded half-space, unlike the wide embeddings where ReLU MLPs normally live.
The damage survives the recurrence: within one model, \texttt{tower\_b} (fed
rectified) scores \num{0.3929} against its \emph{identical twin}
\texttt{tower\_a} (fed raw) at \num{0.4183}, ${\sim}3.9\sigma$ --- and
\texttt{tower\_a} reproduces the standalone plain GRU (\num{0.4235}), which is
exactly what ``the bare tower \emph{rescues}'' requires.

\begin{figure}[ht]
  \centering
  \includegraphics[width=0.8\textwidth]{nexttrack_rectifier}
  \caption{The rectifier mechanism, measured. Next-item genre decodability at
  each representation tap, including one counterfactual the interpretability
  engine cannot express: the model's own affine map with the ReLU deleted. On the
  input side the affine map is \emph{free} (${\sim}0.8\sigma$) and the ReLU is
  the \emph{entire} cost (${\sim}5.9\sigma$); after the recurrence the damage
  persists (${\sim}3.9\sigma$ between two identical towers that differ only in
  whether their input was rectified), while the raw-fed tower reproduces the
  standalone plain GRU. \texttt{frac\_exact\_zero} on the rectified tap
  (\num{0.5327}) matches the affine tap's \texttt{frac\_negative\_coords}
  (\num{0.5327}) to four digits: half-wave rectification of a zero-centred
  latent deletes exactly the negative half, leaving ${\sim}120$ live units for
  192 signed directions.}
  \label{fig:nexttrack-rectifier}
\end{figure}

\emph{Interpretation, and one honest caveat about the controls.} This is the
fifth consecutive architecture null, and it closes the \emph{input side} as the
view null closed the input-view direction: together with the four
learned-combiner nulls, adding representation machinery \emph{around} a single
GRU over a good latent space does not help on this corpus, whereas every real
gain on record came from improving the \emph{latent space itself} (the InfoNCE
content projection, PCA-192) or from the fixed z-blend. The axis is now
quantitatively bounded --- best pre arm \num{0.12369}, a tie; dose flat to twice
the input dimension; placement irrelevant; the fusion-depth interaction
resolved. The caveat is that the \emph{parameter-matched width control was a
weaker instrument than assumed}: C1 (h297) came in at \num{0.11321}, itself
losing to C0, and the batch's width points run \num{176} $\to$ \num{162} $\to$
\num{173} $\to$ \num{179} hits at h256 / h297 / h425 / h454 --- non-monotone,
spanning \num{0.0119} in recall with no trend. ``Single-GRU width is flat on
PCA-192'' survives as a \emph{trend} claim (h454 ties h256), but per-width jitter
is ${\sim}\pm\num{0.01}$ on a single split, roughly two-thirds of the noise band.
\textbf{A width-matched control that is itself a single unreplicated run is not a
reliable capacity anchor at this effect size} --- which is why it matters that
S1's verdict rests on the C0 comparison, reproduced to the last digit. A second
caveat is un-disentangled: the pre-MLP ends in \texttt{Dropout}, so pre arms
receive GRU-\emph{input} dropout that the bare arms lack, and S1's loss could be
partly over-regularization rather than the rectifier --- separating them needs a
\texttt{pre\_dropout} parameter that does not yet exist.
\emph{Source: \texttt{2026-07-26-nexttrack-pre-encoder-scan.md}.}

\section{Next-track: reading the record --- statistical power and the holisticness generations}
\label{sec:nexttrack-power}
Two cross-cutting facts emerged from the 2026-07-25/26 batches that change how
every number above should be read. Neither is a result about a model; both are
results about the \emph{measuring instrument}, and they belong here rather than
buried in one campaign.

\subsection{The nulls are power-limited, and that is a different claim}
\label{sec:nexttrack-power-stats}
Every next-track verdict in this document rests on a paired per-session
bootstrap over the
same \num{1431} test sessions. That $n$ has a measurable resolution. Averaged
over the nine paired comparisons of the pre-encoder batch the 95\% CI half-width
is \textbf{\num{0.0124}} on Recall@10 (\num{0.0128} over the dual-tower batch's
nine, so the figure is stable), and \textbf{${\sim}\num{0.0018}$} on
holisticness@10. Now compare those to the \emph{entire spread} of an architecture
family: \num{0.028} on Recall@10 and \num{0.0031} on holisticness@10
(Table~\ref{tab:nexttrack-power}). The ratio is the whole story --- a family's full range
is only ${\sim}2.3$ resolution widths wide on recall and ${\sim}1.9$ on
holisticness, so of eight adjacent arms roughly \textbf{two tiers} are separable
on Recall@10 and \textbf{one} on holisticness@10. Resolving a \num{0.005}
Recall@10 difference at this precision would require
$n \approx 1431\cdot(\num{0.0124}/\num{0.005})^2 \approx \num{8800}$ test
sessions, six times what the split provides
(Figure~\ref{fig:nexttrack-power}).

\begin{table}[ht]
  \centering
  \caption{Measured resolution of the test split. Half-widths are means over the
  nine paired-bootstrap comparisons of the pre-encoder batch (\num{2000}
  resamples, $n=\num{1431}$); the family spread is that batch's full range on
  each metric. ``Tiers'' is spread\,$/$\,half-width, i.e.\ how many genuinely
  distinguishable levels eight adjacent arms can occupy.
  \emph{Sources: \texttt{2026-07-25-nexttrack-dual-tower-fusion-scan.md},
  \texttt{2026-07-26-nexttrack-pre-encoder-scan.md}.}}
  \label{tab:nexttrack-power}
  {\footnotesize\setlength{\tabcolsep}{6pt}\begin{tabular}{lcccc}
    \toprule
    Metric & CI half-width at $n=\num{1431}$ & family spread & tiers & $n$ for $\Delta=\num{0.005}$ \\
    \midrule
    Recall@10 & 0.0124 & 0.0280 & $\sim$2 of 8 & $\sim$\num{8800} \\
    holisticness@10 & 0.0018 & 0.0031 & $\sim$1 of 8 & --- \\
    \bottomrule
  \end{tabular}}
\end{table}

\begin{figure}[ht]
  \centering
  \includegraphics[width=0.8\textwidth]{nexttrack_power}
  \caption{The architecture nulls are power-limited. The paired-bootstrap CI
  half-width falls as $1/\sqrt{n}$; at the split's $n=\num{1431}$ it is
  \num{0.0124} on Recall@10, while a whole architecture family spans only
  \num{0.028}. Resolving a \num{0.005} difference would need
  ${\sim}\num{8800}$ test sessions. The honest reading of the five architecture
  nulls follows directly: \emph{large harms were detected reliably, moderate wins
  were never detectable at all}.}
  \label{fig:nexttrack-power}
\end{figure}

\textbf{The honest reading of the five architecture nulls follows directly, and
it is not the reading a casual glance gives.} What these campaigns established
firmly is that the tested architectures do not deliver \emph{large} gains, and
that several of them do real \emph{harm} --- the harms are the well-powered
results, because they are big (S1 $-\num{0.0140}$; the \texttt{fusion\_layers}
regression $+\num{0.0266}$; six of eight dual arms below the baseline). What the
campaigns \emph{cannot} establish is the absence of a moderate gain: a genuine
$+\num{0.005}$ improvement --- which on a \num{0.123} baseline would be a
respectable \SI{4}{\percent} relative lift --- would have been invisible by
construction. So ``dual-tower fusion is refuted'' and ``the pre-encoder is
refuted'' should be read as \emph{no large effect, and no effect of the
hypothesized magnitude}, not as ``these architectures do not work.'' The
practical consequence is a prioritization rule rather than a caveat: on this
split, further architecture arms are a poor use of runs no matter how they come
out, because the design space's whole dynamic range is barely twice the
measurement error. Either enlarge $n$ or move to axes whose effects are large ---
which is, empirically, exactly what the representation and supervision axes have
delivered (the content projection's $+\num{0.0259}$ is twice the half-width, and
the second-split hardening's $+\num{0.0426}$ is three times it).

\subsection{Three generations of holisticness@10, and why the board is denominated in recall}
\label{sec:nexttrack-power-generations}
The composite \emph{holisticness@10} was revised twice in four days, and stored
values are \textbf{not comparable across the cuts}:
\begin{description}
  \item[Gen.\ 1 --- 3-factor (to 2026-07-24).] The bare product
  \[\mathrm{clamp}(\text{mood\_coh},0)\times\text{ild}\times(1-\text{artist\_adj}),\]
  carrying \emph{no correctness term at all}. Values land in
  \numrange{0.063}{0.233} --- the column in Table~\ref{tab:nexttrack-dualtower}.
  \item[Gen.\ 2 --- 4-factor, grounded (2026-07-25).] Multiplies in a
  \texttt{music@10} grounding factor,
  \[\mathrm{clamp}\bigl((\text{music@10}-\num{0.185})/\num{0.815},\,0,\,1\bigr).\]
  Because \texttt{music@10} falls in lockstep with recall, this cancels the
  de-eagering ``wins'' the 3-factor form rewarded; values land in
  \numrange{0.044}{0.050} --- the column in Table~\ref{tab:nexttrack-preencoder}.
  \item[Gen.\ 3 --- concentration-aware (2026-07-26).] Eagerness becomes
  \texttt{max(artist\_adj,} \texttt{artist\_conc)} and the grounding floor
  rises \num{0.185}$\to$\num{0.34}. Values land near \numrange{0.009}{0.025}.
\end{description}
Every facet is stored per row, so any generation is recomputable after the fact
(the helper \texttt{rescore\_holisticness.py} does this for the runs it can
reach),
and Table~\ref{tab:nexttrack-hgen} re-derives all three for a representative slice. Two
consequences matter for reading this document. First, \textbf{a holisticness
column must always be labelled with its generation}, because the generation
effect dwarfs the model effect: every 3-factor value mechanically outranks every
4-factor value, since the grounding factor multiplies by ${\sim}0.3$. Second,
and more importantly, \textbf{the recall and holisticness boards point in
opposite directions at every generation}. The champion blend --- \num{0.21174}
Recall@10, \num{303} of \num{1431} --- ranks \emph{last} in
Table~\ref{tab:nexttrack-hgen} under all three formulas, because it is the most
artist-eager model on record; the degenerate \texttt{cummean/cummean} arm and
\texttt{mood-session} lead. Generation 3 demoted \texttt{mood-session} from
first to a genuine zero, but it did \emph{not} fix the dual-tower degenerate arm,
so the $\pm\num{0.015}$ recall floor remains mandatory on any holisticness claim.

  \texttt{2026-07-25-nexttrack-dual-tower-fusion-scan.md},
  \texttt{2026-07-26-nexttrack-pre-encoder-scan.md}.}}
  \label{tab:nexttrack-hgen}
  {\footnotesize\setlength{\tabcolsep}{2.4pt}\begin{tabular}{llccc}

\paragraph{What is primary, and what this document sorts on.} For completeness:
the \texttt{[metrics].primary} key in \texttt{domain.toml}, and the best-models
group's \texttt{primary\_metric}, have both read \texttt{holisticness@10} since
2026-07-23, so the \emph{server-effective} primary metric is no longer
Recall@10. Statements
in the earlier next-track sections that ``the primary metric stays Recall@10''
were true when written and are superseded \emph{for the server}. This document
nevertheless continues to denominate the next-track leaderboard
(Table~\ref{tab:nexttrack-lb}) and every campaign verdict in \textbf{Recall@10},
deliberately: it is the record's comparability currency, it is stable across all
three metric generations, and --- as Table~\ref{tab:nexttrack-hgen} shows --- a
holisticness ordering would place the servable champion last. Holisticness is
reported throughout as a labelled, generation-tagged diagnostic column.
\emph{Sources: \texttt{2026-07-25-nexttrack-dual-tower-fusion-scan.md},
\texttt{2026-07-26-nexttrack-pre-encoder-scan.md}.}

\section{Next-track: the MMR $\lambda$ frontier --- the first confirmed crown lever}
\label{sec:nexttrack-mmr}
\emph{Rationale.} Section~\ref{sec:nexttrack-holisticness} left one standing flag: an
eval-time MMR re-rank at $\lambda\approx\num{0.9}$ was ``the only near-free
holisticness lever on record'', measured in an \emph{offline} sweep over a stored
checkpoint and explicitly deferred to a user decision. This campaign is the
native confirm, and it was designed to settle a second, pre-registered
\emph{mechanism} question rather than merely to re-measure the knob: does sonic
diversification de-concentrate \textbf{artists} (H1, ``responsive''), or are
same-artist tracks far enough apart in the content-metric space that the
diversity penalty leaves eagerness untouched (H2, ``sticky'')? The answer decides
whether an explicit artist-diversity penalty is the indicated sequel or a
redundant one.

\paragraph{What is being maximized.} All ten arms are scored under the
\emph{live} four-factor crown, taken verbatim from
\texttt{predictors/seq\_common.py}:
\[
  \begin{aligned}
    H = {}& \max(\texttt{mood\_coh},0)\;\times\;\texttt{ild}\\
          &\times\;\bigl(1-\mathrm{clamp}(\max(\texttt{artist\_adj},
             \texttt{artist\_conc}),0,1)\bigr)\\
          &\times\;\mathrm{clamp}\bigl((\texttt{music@10}-\num{0.34})/\num{0.66},
             \,0,\,1\bigr),
  \end{aligned}
\]
each facet the \emph{mean over rows} and the product taken \emph{of the
aggregates} --- generation 3 of Section~\ref{sec:nexttrack-power-generations}. Two anchors
frame the batch: \textbf{A}, the recall champion \Rp+M+\Cp\ (H \num{0.008603}, its
eagerness factor bound by \texttt{artist\_conc} \num{0.691653}), and \textbf{B},
the best \emph{single} model on the live crown, \texttt{gru-infonce-h256} (H
\num{0.024538}). The bar to beat is the all-time healthy-arm crown maximum
$H_{\mathrm{bar}} = \num{0.024872}$, held by an h425 capacity control
(\runid{run-20260725-143502-be11b-seq-nexttrack}). All arms ride the canonical
split \dsid{seq-20260715-131139}; no dataset work was needed.

\paragraph{Why this batch is unusually sharp.} $\lambda$ is an \emph{eval-only}
parameter: the trained weights are bit-identical across the arms of one model, so
$\lambda=\num{1.0}$ is a bit-identity gate and every $\Delta$ is a maximally
paired comparison. Both controls reproduced their anchors exactly (\num{303} and
\num{176} hits of \num{1431}, MRR agreeing to \num{3.1e-11}), and every arm's $H$
was \emph{recomputed} from the stored \texttt{predictions.json} under one code
path, so the per-row rounding bias cancels in every $\Delta$ (recomputed versus
stored agreed to \num{7.4e-8} and \num{2.1e-7}). A third gate --- MMR-active ---
required each $\lambda<1$ arm's \texttt{ild} to differ from its own control by far
more than \num{1e-6}, which is the registered detector for MMR silently no-opping
when the content-metric index is unreachable. No arm was a no-op.

\emph{Results.} Table~\ref{tab:nexttrack-mmr-arms} is the frontier, and
Figure~\ref{fig:nexttrack-mmr-frontier} plots it. Arms are tiered by a
pre-registered rule: \textbf{TIER-1 FREE} means $\Delta H$ above zero with the
recall $\Delta$ straddling zero and $|\Delta\text{recall}| \ll \num{0.015}$;
\textbf{TIER-2 PRICED} means a real $H$ gain at a recall cost the CI can see;
\textbf{TIER-3 REJECTED} means $\Delta$recall below $-\num{0.03}$.
