    A3 \emph{t3} & 0.5 / 200 & 0.023576 & $+.0150$ [$+.013$, $+.017$] & 0.16422 (235) & $-.0473$ [$-.061$, $-.033$] & 0.4871 & 0.6527 & 0.486 / 0.461 \\
    A2 \emph{t2} & 0.7 / 200 & 0.015289 & $+.0067$ [$+.006$, $+.008$] & 0.19357 (277) & $-.0181$ [$-.029$, $-.008$] & 0.4759 & 0.5201 & 0.571 / 0.608 \\
    A1 \emph{t1} & 0.9 / 200 & 0.010435 & $+.0018$ [$+.001$, $+.002$] & 0.21034 (301) & $-.0013$ [$-.008$, $+.005$] & 0.4625 & 0.4362 & 0.603 / 0.671 \\
    A0 \emph{ctrl} & 1.0 / 200 & 0.008603 & --- & \best{0.21174 (303)} & --- & 0.4538 & 0.4064 & 0.610 / 0.692 \\
    \bottomrule
  \end{tabular}}
\end{table}

\begin{figure}[ht]
  \centering
  \includegraphics[width=0.95\textwidth]{nexttrack_mmr_frontier}
  \caption{\textbf{Left:} the $\lambda$ frontier on the recall $\times$ crown
  plane. Each engine traces a path as $\lambda$ falls from \num{1.0}; colour is
  the pre-registered tier (green TIER-1 FREE, blue TIER-2 PRICED, red TIER-3
  REJECTED, grey control). Three single-GRU arms clear the prior all-time crown
  bar \num{0.024872} at a recall whose paired CI straddles zero --- the first
  genuinely free crown movement on record. The champion's path reaches comparable
  $H$ only after paying recall the CI can see, and its two best-$H$ points are
  rejected as degenerate. \textbf{Right:} the mechanism question. H1 required
  $|\Delta\texttt{artist\_conc}|\ge|\Delta\texttt{artist\_adj}|$; H2 required the
  ratio below \num{0.5} (shaded). Measured \numrange{1.59}{3.06} in \emph{8 of 8}
  arms, so H2 is refuted by a factor of \numrange{3}{6} in the wrong direction ---
  and the ratio shrinks monotonically as $\lambda$ falls, i.e.\ the easy
  de-concentration is bought first and the mechanism saturates.}
  \label{fig:nexttrack-mmr-frontier}
\end{figure}

\emph{Interpretation.} \textbf{MMR is a real crown lever, and the first one on
record.} Every one of the eight $\lambda<1$ arms lifts $H$ with the CI entirely
above zero. What makes the gain legitimate rather than an artefact of the
composite is that \emph{three} of the four factors improve at once: \texttt{ild}
rises (\num{0.4536}$\to$\num{0.5314} on the GRU, \num{0.4064}$\to$\num{0.8146} on
the champion), eagerness falls, and --- the load-bearing part ---
\texttt{music@10} \emph{also} rises (\num{0.4558}$\to$\num{0.4747}). A lever that
raised $H$ by degrading relevance would be cancelled by the grounding factor; this
one is not, which is precisely the distinction the floor was written to enforce
after the \texttt{cummean/cummean} and \texttt{seq-mood} pathologies.

\textbf{H1 is confirmed unanimously and H2 is refuted, which \emph{cancels} the
sequel the previous campaign had pre-registered.} In 8 of 8 arms
$\Delta\texttt{artist\_conc} \le \Delta\texttt{artist\_adj}$ (both negative) at a
ratio of \numrange{1.59}{3.06}. Same-artist tracks \emph{are} near-neighbours in
the content-metric space, so a max-similarity penalty de-concentrates artists as a
side effect, with no explicit notion of artist anywhere in it. Sonic MMR
therefore \emph{is} a de-eagering lever, and porting \texttt{seq-mood}'s explicit
\texttt{artist\_penalty} to the blend --- the pre-registered H2 follow-up --- is no
longer the indicated next move. It stays on file as a \emph{complement}, because
\texttt{artist\_adj} falls ${\sim}\num{2.5}\times$ slower than
\texttt{artist\_conc} and becomes the binding facet at low $\lambda$.

\textbf{The binding facet switches, and that locates the end of the lever.} On the
champion the switch from \texttt{artist\_conc} to \texttt{artist\_adj} happens
between $\lambda\num{0.7}$ and $\lambda\num{0.5}$; on the GRU it has already
happened by $\lambda\num{0.9}$. Once \texttt{artist\_adj} binds, the
$(1-\text{eager})$ factor is the stiff one and each further recall point buys
progressively less crown --- the structural reason the GRU's frontier flattens
where it does, and a prediction that pushing below ${\sim}\lambda\num{0.7}$ on the
GRU is poor value. B3 is the confirmation: the highest $H$ measured anywhere
(\num{0.034171}) at the cost of losing the recall tie.

\textbf{The promotable operating point is the pool probe.} \textbf{B4}
($\lambda\num{0.7}$, \texttt{mmr\_pool}~\num{50}) is the single best TIER-1 arm at
$H$~\num{0.030355} --- $+\SI{22.1}{\percent}$ over $H_{\mathrm{bar}}$ and
\num{5.4} measured half-widths clear of it --- and it beats B2 on $H$ by
$+\num{0.000889}$ with the CI above zero at a recall that straddles, so its rank
is \emph{resolved} rather than nominal. B2 ($\lambda\num{0.8}$) was the design's
pre-registered promotion hypothesis and fired almost exactly as projected
($\num{0.0321}$ predicted against \num{0.029458} measured, with a \emph{smaller}
recall cost than projected). Nothing was promoted: six definitions were
registered, each carrying the note ``NOT PROMOTED --- robustness confirm $+$ user
approval pending''. The recall board is untouched --- \textbf{no arm improves
recall}; MMR only ever costs it.

\textbf{The second axis, \texttt{mmr\_pool}, closes as a fine-tuning knob rather
than an axis.} Capping the candidate pool at \num{50} recovers recall
($\Delta$ vs pool~\num{200} $+\num{0.005584}$, CI above zero, as hypothesized ---
a cap bounds how far MMR can reach for diversity) but gives $H$ back
($-\num{0.003838}$, CI below zero). It does not win at fixed $\lambda$; it moves
along the \emph{same} frontier. What earns it a line in the record is the
cross-comparison above: the cap reaches a slightly \emph{better} point than the
nearest pure-$\lambda$ setting. Prefer $\lambda$ for coarse movement, pool for
fine-tuning at a chosen $\lambda$.

\textbf{The champion/GRU contrast resolves in favour of factor health, not
de-eagering headroom.} The design framed this as the campaign-carrying question:
the champion is $\num{1.9}\times$ more eager, so a given absolute de-eagering is
worth ${\sim}\num{2.1}\times$ more relative $H$ there --- yet it starts
$\num{2.85}\times$ behind, and the gap \emph{widens} under diversification
($H$~\num{0.015289} against \num{0.034171} at $\lambda\num{0.7}$). The facets say
why: the champion's \texttt{mood\_coh} \textbf{collapses} as it diversifies
(\num{0.3982}$\to$\num{0.2378} at $\lambda\num{0.3}$, $-\SI{40}{\percent}$) while
the GRU's barely moves ($-\SI{4}{\percent}$). The champion's recall comes from
artist-adjacency --- the Markov bigram leg, per
Section~\ref{sec:nexttrack-holisticness} --- so diversifying away from the seed artist also
walks it out of the prefix's mood neighbourhood. It pays twice. \textbf{MMR's
value scales with the health of the other three factors, not with room to
de-eager}, and no $\lambda$ closes the champion's crown gap.

\paragraph{Two instrument findings, one of them a warning about the record
itself.} First, \textbf{the shared-weights instrument is
$\numrange{3}{33}\times$ sharper than the architecture-family noise floor}:
measured half-widths run \numrange{0.000382}{0.002476} on $\Delta H$ against the
family's conservative \num{0.0018}, with the number of sessions whose hit@10
changed at all as low as \textbf{\num{9}}. Arm A1 had been pre-registered as
``likely UNRESOLVABLE'' and resolved at \num{4.8} measured half-widths.
Eval-time levers must therefore budget their minimum detectable effect from the
\emph{pairing}, not from the architecture figure of
Section~\ref{sec:nexttrack-power-stats} --- the two regimes differ by more than an order of
magnitude, and conflating them under-powers a whole class of cheap experiments.
The corollary is reassuring in the other direction: the 2026-07-22 \emph{offline}
$\lambda$ sweep projected recall \num{0.2103} / \num{0.1936} / \num{0.1642} /
\num{0.1097} and the native runs returned \num{0.21034} / \num{0.19357} /
\num{0.16422} / \num{0.10971} --- four for four to four decimals, which validates
the offline driver as a \emph{screening} instrument.

Second, a new infrastructure pitfall was found the hard way and it retroactively
qualifies part of the record: \textbf{a remote worker running a
\texttt{(deleted)} binary image silently strips metric keys its struct does not
know}. The metric-completeness gate failed on the first attempt with
\texttt{artist\_conc\_at\_k} missing, although the predictors were md5-identical
to the hub and the worker's own \texttt{metrics.json} carried the field: the
worker \emph{daemon} had started a day before the field existed, \texttt{rsync}
had replaced the file on disk but not the running image, and the old image
deserialized the predictor's metrics into a struct lacking the field, dropped it,
and posted the filtered set \textbf{without erroring}. \texttt{holisticness@10}
was nevertheless correct throughout, because the predictor computes $H$ itself
before the server sees it --- which is exactly what made the defect invisible.
Every remote run in a four-hour window on 2026-07-26 lost
\texttt{artist\_conc\_at\_k}, which is why anchor B's quoted concentration was a
\emph{reconstruction} from \texttt{top\_k\_ids} and the dataset artist map. That
reconstruction is now independently validated: the natively emitted values
(\num{0.6916530786551751} and \num{0.35591272614333413}) match the rescoring
tool's reconstructions to the last digit. Remedy: cycle the daemon whenever a
metric field is added; \texttt{rsync} of the binary is not sufficient.
\emph{Source: \texttt{2026-07-26-nexttrack-mmr-lambda-frontier.md}.}

\section{Next-track: autoregressive rollouts: a generation metric, and five sweeps that say no}
\label{sec:nexttrack-rollout}
\emph{Rationale.} This campaign was provoked by a \emph{user preference}, not by a
metric. On a real 135-track playlist the user strongly preferred one engine's
20-step journey over another's, while \texttt{holisticness@10} scored those two
journeys \num{0.003} apart. That gap is the point: every metric on record ---
Recall@10, holisticness@10 and its four facets, \texttt{music@10}, even the
multi-step continuation evaluator --- scores \emph{one} ranked list produced from a
\emph{real} prefix. None of them ever feeds a model its own output. A 20-step
journey does exactly that, so a trajectory failure --- a rollout whose energy
decays until a party seed has become a comedown --- is structurally invisible to
all of them. This is the first campaign in this document to build an instrument
for the \emph{deployment} regime, and it is the direct ancestor of the walk
surface of Section~\ref{sec:nexttrack-artistcap}.

\paragraph{What was built.} Four things, all off the training path.
\begin{description}
  \item[\texttt{hold@N}, a generation metric.] Per legible axis $a$ in
  \{energy, valence, acousticness, tempo\},
  $\texttt{fade}_a = |\mathrm{mean}(\text{2nd half}) -
  \mathrm{mean}(\text{1st half})|/\texttt{band}_a$, and then
  \[
    \texttt{hold@}N = 1 - \mathrm{clamp}\bigl(\mathrm{mean}_a(\texttt{fade}_a)
      + \texttt{stasis},\,0,\,1\bigr).
  \]
  The per-axis bands are calibrated on \num{2135} real
  \emph{train} sessions (energy fade p10 \num{0.0141}, p50 \num{0.0769}, p90
  \num{0.1963}; tempo \num{1.28}, \num{8.05}, \num{22.43}), so axes with
  incompatible units combine and ``a big fade'' means big \emph{relative to how
  this listener actually listens}. Real sessions are not flat --- the median energy
  fade is \num{0.077} --- so a metric rewarding zero fade would rank an
  unnaturally rigid generator above the listener it imitates.
  \item[\texttt{stasis}, the anti-gaming term.] A pure fade metric is
  degenerate: twenty near-identical tracks fade by \num{0} and score \num{1.0},
  which is the
  same pathology that forces \texttt{holisticness@10} to carry an \emph{external}
  recall floor. \texttt{stasis} puts the floor \emph{inside} the metric by
  penalizing steps smaller than the listener's own p10 jump, and it was verified
  on a synthetic flatline (moves-naturally \num{1.000}, at-your-floor \num{1.000},
  half-floor \num{0.500}, barely-moves \num{0.048}).
  \item[An offline rollout evaluator.] \texttt{seq\_continuation\_eval.py
  --rollout} scores sampled \emph{test} sessions by \textbf{importing} the
  generation loop from the serving path rather than reimplementing it, so an
  offline score describes the journeys the lab actually serves; the refactor was
  verified byte-identical. It is on-demand and sampled because a rollout costs
  \texttt{steps} full-vocabulary passes per session against the primary path's one
  (${\sim}20\times$).
  \item[A playlist-lab readout.] A mood report card, a comparative journey-shape
  chart and per-step \texttt{passed\_over} diagnostics, with the holisticness
  facets demoted to a collapsed detail block.
\end{description}

\emph{Results.} Table~\ref{tab:nexttrack-rollout} is five sweeps, \num{120} paired
rollouts and \num{240} journeys, under a paired bootstrap over \emph{seeds} plus a
leave-one-seed-out jackknife. Rollout policy was frozen throughout at the lab
defaults (\num{20} steps, artist penalty \num{2}, anchor \num{1}, temperature
\num{0} --- deterministic, so one seed is one sample and there is no rollout
variance).

\begin{table}[ht]
  \centering
  \caption{Five \texttt{hold@20} sweeps, paired bootstrap over seeds (\num{2000}
  resamples, rng \num{1337}) with a leave-one-seed-out jackknife (``LOO'' is the
  number of refits preserving the verdict). Positive $\Delta$ favours the
  dual-tower arm. The pre-registered rule was CONFIRMED iff the CI lies entirely
  above zero \emph{and} the verdict survives $\ge\SI{90}{\percent}$ of LOO refits;
  TIE iff the CI straddles. S2 and S3 met that rule and were nonetheless
  \emph{retracted} --- see the interpretation.
  \emph{Source: \texttt{2026-07-27-rollout-hold-metric.md}.}}
  \label{tab:nexttrack-rollout}
  {\footnotesize\setlength{\tabcolsep}{4pt}\begin{tabular}{llrlll}
    \toprule
    \# & Sweep & $n$ & $\Delta$\,\texttt{hold@20} [95\% CI] & LOO & Verdict \\
    \midrule
    S1 & pilot, mood-extreme seeds, 10-track prefix & 8 & $-0.034$ [$-0.138$, $+0.079$] & 0/8 & TIE \\
    S2 & pilot, mood-extreme seeds, 30-track prefix & 8 & $+0.314$ [$+0.107$, $+0.529$] & 8/8 & \emph{retracted} \\
    S3 & replication, \textbf{random} seeds & 39 & $+0.122$ [$+0.044$, $+0.201$] & 39/39 & \emph{confounded} \\
    S4 & \textbf{same-split control} & 39 & $+0.033$ [$-0.030$, $+0.093$] & 0/39 & \best{TIE} \\
    S4b & same-split, other dual checkpoint & 39 & $+0.051$ [$-0.012$, $+0.118$] & 0/39 & \best{TIE} \\
    S4c & \textbf{within-architecture} & 39 & $-0.072$ [$-0.141$, $-0.002$] & --- & significant \\
    S5 & \textbf{the user's own \num{11} playlists} & 11 & $+0.004$ [$-0.088$, $+0.104$] & 0/11 & \best{TIE} \\
    --- & the single judged playlist & 1 & $+0.379$ (\num{0.623} / \num{0.245}) & --- & outlier \\
    \bottomrule
  \end{tabular}}
\end{table}

\emph{Interpretation.} \textbf{Rollout fade does not separate this family.} At
matched training split the two architectures tie \emph{twice}; on the user's own
playlists the pair they preferred ties; and the only surviving effect is
\textbf{checkpoint-level} within one architecture (S4c), which does not transfer to
the playlist regime. Mean \texttt{hold@20} over the identical \num{39} random
seeds runs \num{0.6488} / \num{0.6309} for the two dual-tower checkpoints against
\num{0.5983} / \num{0.5266} for the two single-GRU ones --- so S3's apparent
architecture win decomposes into a non-significant architecture component
($+\num{0.05}$) and a significant \emph{checkpoint} component ($+\num{0.072}$).
Two \texttt{seq-nexttrack} models differ from each other more than dual-tower
differs from single-GRU at matched split, and the checkpoint the playlist lab
pre-selected by default happens to be the worst holder of the four. The user's
original comparison was the best holder against the worst.

Combined with the five prior architecture nulls this is a sixth, and it is the
strongest form available, because the standing rebuttal to those nulls was
``Recall@10 cannot see session quality''. It cannot; and when an instrument built
for the deployment regime looks, there is still nothing there.

\textbf{Per-seed variance dominates model identity, and that is the operational
finding.} Individual gaps on \emph{one} model pair span $+\num{0.39}$ to
$-\num{0.27}$, while the model-level mean difference on the user's playlists is
$+\num{0.004}$. Which seed you hand the generator matters far more than which of
these models generates. Two consequences: prefer serving an \emph{ensemble} and
letting the listener choose over hunting a single better generator, and budget
$\ge\num{40}$ seeds for any future rollout comparison --- \numrange{8}{12} cannot
resolve anything at this variance.

\textbf{\texttt{hold@N} is a working instrument but not a model ranker.} It fired
on a real checkpoint difference, it fired on the judged playlist (\num{0.62}
against \num{0.25}) where \texttt{holisticness@10} saw a \num{0.003} gap, and it
correctly returned TIE three times. The recommendation is therefore to keep it as
a per-journey \emph{lab readout} and \textbf{not} to wire it as a leaderboard
column: its ground truth is listener preference, which stands at $n=1$ blind vote.
Promoting an unvalidated composite to primary is how the crown ended up needing an
external recall floor, and replacing one unvalidated composite with another repeats
the mistake.

\paragraph{Four methodological pitfalls, each of which cost a retracted
positive.} They are worth more than the null. \textbf{(1) A cross-split comparison
masquerading as an architecture result.} The lab's two default pre-selects are
trained on \emph{different} splits with identical vocab and item latents, so
comparing them attributes checkpoint and split differences to architecture; pair
from the same batch when the claim is architectural. \textbf{(2) Mood-extreme seed
selection inflates effects ${\sim}\num{2.5}\times$} (S2's $+\num{0.314}$ became
S3's $+\num{0.122}$ on a random sample). \textbf{(3) An anti-gaming term can be
too weak to do its job:} \texttt{stasis} first normalized the shortfall by the
band \emph{width}, and because this listener's jump band is wide a flatlined
rollout scored \num{0.740} --- \emph{above} a real generator's \num{0.477}.
Normalize by the \emph{floor}; a guard beatable by the degeneracy it targets
launders that degeneracy as a good score. \textbf{(4) A metric that returns
nothing must drop the \emph{pair}} and report the smaller $n$, or the pairing
silently breaks. The jackknife earned its keep at $n<40$ throughout: it killed two
of three per-axis positives at $n=8$ and flagged S4 and S5 as unsupported at 0/39
and 0/11.

\paragraph{What the per-axis read adds, including a failed negative control.} At
$n=\num{39}$ only \emph{energy} fade separates the arms ($-\num{0.039}$
[$-\num{0.069}$, $-\num{0.009}$], favouring dual-tower); valence, acousticness and
tempo all straddle. But one pre-registered \emph{negative} control moved: the
energy \emph{level} deviation from the seed favours the single GRU
($+\num{0.018}$ [$+\num{0.005}$, $+\num{0.032}$]). Those are different properties,
so the claim had to be re-scoped from ``holds the seed better'' to ``is more
stationary'' --- and it shows that \texttt{hold@N}'s deliberate exclusion of a level
term is a choice that \emph{changes the ranking}, not a neutral simplification.
Two other limits travel with the batch: \SI{74}{\percent} of the corpus carries
the acoustic fields the metric needs, so seeds were pre-filtered for
$\ge\SI{60}{\percent}$ prefix coverage (a mild selection effect), and nothing here
has been listened to.

Nothing was promoted, registered or wired; the champion, the crown board and the
best-models pins are unchanged. For the record, the live-crown standing relevant
to the dual-tower question is a \emph{tie} rather than a loss --- the best
legitimate dual arm \num{0.02378} against the single GRU's \num{0.02454}, a
\num{0.0008} gap against a ${\sim}\num{0.0018}$ half-width. The family was refuted
on Recall@10, ties on the live primary, and shows nothing on the rollout axis.
The indicated next axis is therefore not topology but the \emph{training regime}:
all five architecture nulls held one-step teacher forcing byte-identical while the
product rolls twenty steps on its own output, and that exposure-bias mismatch is
the one thing those nulls could not have detected.
\emph{Source: \texttt{2026-07-27-rollout-hold-metric.md}.}

\section{Next-track: a research memo, and what its provenance permits}
\label{sec:nexttrack-memo}
\emph{Status, first.} This section consolidates a document that is \textbf{not a
campaign report}. Nothing was run on the server, nothing was promoted, nothing was
registered; the champion, the leaderboard, the crown board and the best-models
pins are all unchanged by it. It was produced by a research workflow (four scouts,
five proposal lenses, five adversarial screeners, then synthesis) plus
\emph{offline} scoring against frozen checkpoints. Where it reports a number, that
number came from an offline screen over stored artifacts, not from a native run.
It is included here because it is the direct parent of the campaign in
Section~\ref{sec:nexttrack-markovgate} and because its negative results --- the graveyard
below --- are cheaper to reuse than to rediscover. It is reported at a lower
register than the measured campaigns, and its figures are deliberately kept out of
every board in this document.

\paragraph{The provenance partition, kept visible.} The memo itself distinguishes
two classes of number, and that distinction survives into this synthesis.
\begin{description}
  \item[Independently re-derived by the orchestrator.] The share of test queries
  whose last prefix item has \emph{zero} train-bigram support:
  \textbf{\num{795} of \num{1431} $=$ \num{0.5556}}, reproduced by an independent
  script. The ungated control, which is self-validating because it must equal the
  champion: \textbf{\num{0.21174}}, exact. The gated arm: \textbf{\num{0.23201}},
  a paired $\Delta$ of $+\num{0.02027}$ $[+\num{0.01048}, +\num{0.03075}]$ with the
  CI above zero. And its bucket decomposition: the zero-support bucket moves
  \num{0.1660}$\to$\num{0.2025} over \num{795} queries while the two warm buckets
  are \textbf{bit-identical} (\num{0.3116} and \num{0.2326}, unchanged).
  \item[\textbf{Not} verified by the orchestrator --- agent-reported only, treated
  as provisional.] The $+\num{0.01957}$ second-split figure, the replay channel's
  $+\num{0.02865}$, the \texttt{latent\_gamma} numbers, and the recomputed
  \num{0.0706} discordance with its ${\sim}\num{0.0145}$ noise floor. None of these
  appears in Table~\ref{tab:nexttrack-lb} or on any crown board, and the first of them is
  precisely what the follow-up campaign was commissioned to establish natively.
\end{description}

\paragraph{One caveat the memo states about its own headline.} The ten gating
variants were screened \textbf{on the test set}, so quoting the best arm's CI is
optimistic by construction and the screen cannot itself be evidence. Three things
mitigate it --- four mechanistically related variants all land at
\numrange{0.019}{0.020}, the bucket decomposition is a mechanical explanation
rather than a fit, and the warm buckets do not move by a single hit --- but the
mitigation is an argument, not a measurement. The native two-split confirm is the
real gate, and it is Section~\ref{sec:nexttrack-markovgate}.

\paragraph{The honest headline: ``a better topology'' is the wrong question.} The
record had by then answered it six times --- cell and loss on the AE-64 space;
depth, width and direction; width on PCA-192; dual-tower fusion
(Section~\ref{sec:nexttrack-dualtower}); the MLP fusion head, an \emph{active} regression
recovered by deleting it; the per-step pre-encoder, a measured loss with the
mechanism identified down to \texttt{frac\_exact\_zero} $=$
\texttt{frac\_negative\_coords} (Section~\ref{sec:nexttrack-preencoder}); and rollout fade
(Section~\ref{sec:nexttrack-rollout}). Five lenses were pointed at the topology question
and \emph{every} architecture-shaped proposal came back weakened, sub-noise, or
pre-killed. Two came back with measured negative results: a split Markov leg lost
at every point of its own pre-registered grid, and a factored artist softmax head
was killed by its own pre-kill run, which found that top-5 artist gating on the
real checkpoint changes recall by \textbf{exactly \num{0.0}} because the trained
GRU's top-10 already lives inside its own top-5 artists.

\textbf{Where the evidence points instead: the model is not the constraint. What
it is asked to rank against, and which legs are allowed to speak on a given
query, are.} Every proposal that survived adversarial screening is a candidate-set
or evidence-conditioning change --- an evidence-gated Markov leg, a causal
cross-session replay channel, per-leg reliability scalars, a prefix-only negative
mask, the never-swept softmax temperature. None is a topology. This is the same
conclusion the record had reached from the representation side, arrived at from
five independent directions, and it comes with a measurement rationale: eval-time
and score-time levers are $\numrange{3}{33}\times$ cheaper to resolve than
training-time ones (Section~\ref{sec:nexttrack-mmr}), so a shortlist of score-time
proposals is the rational response to the instrument this lab has.

\paragraph{The two survivors worth naming.} \textbf{(1) \texttt{markov\_gate}} ---
gate the Markov leg to abstain where it has no bigram evidence, restoring the
\texttt{trust} term that the blend's z-normalization erases. Twelve lines,
zero learned parameters, and confirmed natively in the next section.
\textbf{(2) A causal cross-session replay channel} --- \SI{76.17}{\percent} of the
held-out targets were played in a \emph{strictly earlier} session and are not in
the current prefix, and nothing in the family can see this, because the Markov leg
is a train-frozen bigram and the recency scorer only ever scored prefix items,
which the evaluator masks to $-\infty$ (its recorded \num{0.001} is dead code, not
a measurement). Its hit-Jaccard with the GRU is \num{0.060} against \num{0.201}
for the GRU/Markov pair that produced a working blend, i.e.\ it is genuinely
different signal. Its catch is \emph{governance}, not code: it is an
information-set amendment that moves the rankable ceiling from \num{0.4493} to
\num{0.7617}, which needs its own board column and an explicit decision before any
number from it is comparable with anything in this document. A third proposal,
\texttt{latent\_gamma} (a one-scalar spectral rescale of the item latents), is
worth recording for its \emph{reframe} rather than its delta: on the offline
screen the champion's entire recall contribution is reproducible by a
\num{192}-number diagonal rescale, which recasts ``the single biggest win on
record'' as a conditioning fix that supervision happened to find, and predicts
that further linear metric learning is near-exhausted. All of these figures are in
the provisional class.

\paragraph{The graveyard, which is the reusable part.} Each entry is a lane that
should not be re-opened without new information. Full-catalogue or
support-restricted cross-entropy is pre-empted by an existing arm at
${\sim}\SI{53}{\percent}$ support coverage (\num{0.1223} against \num{0.1230}) ---
the literature's top candidate does not transfer because the item side is frozen.
A factored artist softmax head is refuted by its own pre-kill (exactly \num{0.0}).
Splitting the Markov leg into track and back-off components was measured at every
grid point and lost at all five ($-\num{0.0217}$ to $-\num{0.0384}$, all CIs below
zero), because separate z-normalization structurally destroys the \texttt{trust}
term inside the warm regime. A pool-matched stage-2 re-ranker is unobtainable: the
train pool's positive density is \num{0.5975} against test's \num{0.3368}, so its
own go-gate passes \emph{because of} the confound. A mixture-of-vMF head loses to
its own oracle decomposition. Diffusion, DreamRec and flow-matching are
externally falsified for this setting, and generative retrieval with semantic IDs
is strictly dominated when exact cosine over \SI{15}{\mega\byte} is already
affordable and the corpus is \SIrange{55}{64}{\percent} cold. Popularity
debiasing has nothing to debias (the popularity baseline is \num{0.0007}).
Hard-negative mining is harmful at low temperature here, because the hardest
negatives are same-album and same-artist tracks --- i.e.\ mining false positives.
And the GRU's pre-normalization output norm is not a confidence signal
(correlation with correctness $+\num{0.0073}$, and the loss is exactly
norm-invariant); the top-1-minus-next-10 score margin is, at $+\num{0.294}$.

\paragraph{The one durable theoretical result.} The memo's cross-cutting
observation that \textbf{the learned-combiner anomaly is mechanically explained}
is the part that changes how the record should be read. A prior oracle search over
\emph{global static scalar} leg weights returned $+\num{0.004}$ straddling zero,
and it is now clear why that is compatible with a real $+\num{0.020}$ from a
gate: dropping the Markov leg \emph{globally} gains in the zero-support bucket and
loses in the warm bucket, and the two cancel almost exactly. No constant can
express an intervention whose sign flips with a per-query data statistic, and
z-normalization is scale-invariant so it provably cannot see one. The registered
``four independent learned-combiner nulls'' pitfall should therefore forbid
\emph{learned score-level fusion} --- fitted weights, stackers, rank fusion,
end-to-end representation fusion --- and \textbf{not} per-query evidence gating,
which is a third thing: nothing is learned, nothing co-adapts, and the condition
is a train-side count.
\emph{Source: \texttt{2026-07-27b-nexttrack-model-topology-research-memo.md}
(research memo; see the provenance partition above).}

\section{Next-track: \texttt{markov\_gate}: a leg that abstains where it has no evidence}
\label{sec:nexttrack-markovgate}
\emph{Rationale.} This is the native two-split confirm of the memo's headline
proposal (Section~\ref{sec:nexttrack-memo}), and it produces the largest single recall gain
in the whole next-track arc from a nine-line change with no new parameters.

\paragraph{The mechanism, and why it is not the refuted combiner lane.} The Markov
leg mixes its bigram row with an artist/genre back-off through a trust weight
$\texttt{trust} = n_u/(n_u+8)$, where $n_u$ is the train-level bigram support of
the query's last prefix item $u$. At $n_u = 0$ the trust is zero and the leg is
\textbf{pure back-off}: it carries no track-level evidence at all. But the blend's
candidate normalizer is \emph{scale-invariant} by construction --- it maps each
leg's score vector to unit variance so that three legs on wildly different scales
are commensurable --- so the evidence-free back-off is re-inflated and speaks at a
full one-third weight anyway. On the canonical split that happens on \textbf{\num{795}
of \num{1431} test queries}, \SI{55.6}{\percent}. The intervention is to make the
leg \emph{abstain} at $n_u = 0$ and renormalize the divisor from three legs to two.
Nothing is learned; the condition is a train-side count. That is exactly the third
category the registered learned-combiner pitfall did not cover, and the bucket
decomposition below is a mechanical proof that no global static weight could find
the effect: dropping the leg globally is a $-\num{0.0070}$ tie, because the
zero-support gain and the warm-bucket loss cancel.

\paragraph{Design and the preflight gate.} Six runs on the hub, all
\texttt{seq-blend} from the champion definition, with the controls
\textbf{re-trained in this batch} rather than substituted from the archive so that
every paired comparison is code-matched to one generation. Two splits: the
canonical \dsid{seq-20260715-131139} and the disjoint earlier-holdout
\dsid{seq-20260718-211238} (\SI{64}{\percent} cold, $n_{\mathrm{test}}$
\num{1431}). The hard pre-registered stop was that the ungated control had to
reproduce Recall@10 \num{0.21174}, exactly \num{303} of \num{1431}, proving the new
flag left the champion bit-identical. \textbf{It did, to the last digit}
(\num{0.21174004192872117}).

\emph{Results.} Table~\ref{tab:nexttrack-markovgate} is the batch,
Table~\ref{tab:nexttrack-markovgate-paired} the paired statistics, and
Table~\ref{tab:nexttrack-markovgate-buckets} the decomposition that carries the argument.
Figure~\ref{fig:nexttrack-markov-gate} plots both.

\begin{figure}[ht]
  \centering
  \includegraphics[width=0.95\textwidth]{nexttrack_markov_gate}
  \caption{\textbf{Left:} the bucket decomposition on all three control/arm pairs

  \texttt{ds1 $\lambda$0.7} the same canonical pair under an MMR re-rank). The
  entire effect sits in the zero-support bucket; in both warm buckets the arms'
  top-$k$ lists are byte-for-byte identical, which is why a global leg weight
  provably cannot express this intervention. \textbf{Right:} the same result on the
  recall $\times$ crown plane. MMR alone (blue) is a \emph{priced} lever on the
  champion --- it buys crown and loses recall with the CI below zero. The gate
  (green) buys \emph{both}. Composed, gate$+$MMR lands at champion recall
  ($\Delta$ $+\num{0.00140}$, straddling) with $\num{3.6}\times$ the champion's
  holisticness, so the gate changes MMR's tier on the champion from PRICED to FREE.}
  \label{fig:nexttrack-markov-gate}
\end{figure}

\emph{Interpretation.} \textbf{The defect is real, it belongs to the normalizer,
and it is worth \num{29} hits.} Scale-invariance is exactly what makes the z-blend
robust to legs on incommensurable scales, and exactly what destroys their
\emph{confidence}. A Markov leg that has never seen the current track emits a pure
artist/genre back-off; the normalizer re-inflates it to the same footprint as a GRU
that has actually modelled the prefix, and the blend averages the two as equals.
Turning that third of the vote off where it is uninformed converts \num{43} misses
into hits and \num{14} hits into misses --- net $+\num{29}$, $+\SI{9.6}{\percent}$
relative, on the best model on record. Recall@10 \num{0.23201} (\num{332} of
\num{1431}) is \textbf{the best recall figure in this document}, with the paired CI
above zero on two disjoint splits and MRR up on both. For scale: the champion's own
coronation margin over the prior crown was $+\num{0.0259}$ canonical and
$+\num{0.0426}$ on the second split; this gate adds $+\num{0.0203}$ /
$+\num{0.0196}$ \emph{on top of that}, at no additional training cost.

\textbf{The gate is also a crown lever, which was not predicted.} $\Delta H$ is
above zero in all three paired comparisons, and the facets say why: abstaining
kills the \emph{bigram-echo} component of the recommendation. Artist concentration
falls \num{0.69165}$\to$\num{0.59821} and adjacency \num{0.60971}$\to$\num{0.57477}
--- the list stops repeating one artist --- while \texttt{mood\_coh} \emph{rises}
\num{0.39822}$\to$\num{0.46226} and \texttt{music@10} rises
\num{0.45379}$\to$\num{0.49319}. All four crown factors move the right way at
once. This is the first lever on record that de-eagers the champion \emph{without}
paying in mood coherence, and it resolves the exact double-payment
Section~\ref{sec:nexttrack-mmr} identified as structural: there, de-eagering was a re-rank
fighting the model's own scores, so walking away from the seed artist also walked
the blend out of the prefix's mood neighbourhood. Here the de-eagering is the
removal of a vote that had no business being cast.

\textbf{Gate and MMR are additive, not substitutive, and their composition is the
headline for the crown board.} The gate-alone $\Delta$ ($+\num{0.02027}$) and the
gate-under-$\lambda\num{0.7}$ $\Delta$ ($+\num{0.01957}$) are
indistinguishable, and the buckets show why: MMR re-ranks the top of a scored list,
the gate changes which list gets scored, and they act on disjoint parts of the
pipeline. Standing alone, MMR $\lambda\num{0.7}$ on the champion is TIER-2 PRICED
($-\num{0.01817}$ recall, CI below zero). Add the gate and the recall cost is paid
back exactly: A3 is $+\num{0.00140}$ $[-\num{0.01188}, +\num{0.01468}]$, a
statistical tie with the champion, at $H$~\num{0.030980} --- $\num{3.6}\times$ the
champion's \num{0.008603}, comfortably above the mandatory \num{0.108} recall
floor. On the crown board that sits at \textbf{rank 2}, behind only the
$\lambda\num{0.7}$ single GRU, which scores its \num{0.034171} at recall
\num{0.11461}: A3 delivers \SI{96}{\percent} of the incumbent leader's crown at
$\num{1.86}\times$ its recall.

\textbf{The honest caveat, and why the out-of-sample structure redeems it.} The
memo screened ten gating variants on the test set, so its banked
$+\num{0.02027}$ was selection-optimistic and could not be evidence. Four things
redeem it here: the effect reproduces on a \emph{second, disjoint,
\SI{64}{\percent}-cold split that was not screened on}, within \num{0.0007} of the
canonical figure; it reproduces under a third condition ($\lambda\num{0.7}$) that
was not screened either; four mechanistically related variants in the memo all
landed at \numrange{0.019}{0.020}, so the screen was measuring a plateau rather
than selecting a lucky corner; and the warm buckets do not move by a single hit,
which is a \emph{structural} prediction of the mechanism rather than a fitted
outcome. One residual risk remains: the $n_u = 0$ \emph{boundary} itself was chosen
with test knowledge. The boundary-free version --- scaling the leg by
\texttt{trust} \emph{before} normalization instead of hard-abstaining --- is the top
follow-up, and it is strictly more general. Nor is any of this a fluke of one
split's cold rate: the second split is \SI{64}{\percent} cold, its zero-support
bucket grows to \SI{62.3}{\percent} of queries while the per-query gain shrinks
slightly, and the net $\Delta$ comes out nearly identical. The lever's value scales
with how much of the query distribution has no bigram evidence, which is the
majority on any split of this corpus.

\paragraph{Governance, and an infrastructure finding that nearly ate the result.}
Nothing was promoted, registered or tagged; by design boundary this campaign
carries a promotion \emph{recommendation} only, and the board row in
Table~\ref{tab:nexttrack-lb} is labelled accordingly. The server's deterministic
top-12-by-holisticness recompute nevertheless auto-promoted four of the batch's
runs, which is server behaviour rather than a campaign action and left the group
needing re-curation. Separately: the remote training worker was live with a
\textbf{stale checkout containing zero occurrences of the new flag}. Had any gated
arm been dispatched there it would have executed the \emph{ungated} path and
silently returned a null --- the registered ``remote checkout can silently diverge''
pitfall, in its worst form, because the failure is a wrong number rather than a
crash. It could not happen here only because remote dispatch is opt-in and every
run in this batch was posted without it (confirmed by an unclaimed marker on all
six). Until the checkout is freshened, any queued \texttt{seq-blend} run silently
ignores the gate.

\paragraph{Record amendments.} Two, both narrowing rather than overturning.
\textbf{(1)} The learned-combiner pitfall should forbid \emph{learned score-level
fusion} and not \emph{per-query evidence gating}; the prior oracle null and this
$+\num{0.020}$ win are both true and are reconciled by the bucket decomposition,
since a global static scalar cannot express a gate whose sign flips with $n_u$.
\textbf{(2)} The normalizer's erasure of per-leg \emph{confidence} is a
\textbf{general} defect, of which the Markov leg's zero-support back-off is one
instance. Any leg with a query-dependent notion of ``I have no evidence here'' is
mis-weighted the same way, and the content and GRU legs have not been audited for
it.
\emph{Source: \texttt{2026-07-27c-nexttrack-markov-gate-confirm.md}.}

\section{Next-track: the eagerness regularizer at a calibrated margin: refuted, with a mechanism}
\label{sec:nexttrack-eagermargin}
\emph{Rationale.} Section~\ref{sec:nexttrack-holisticness} left \texttt{eager\_beta} --- the
only \emph{training-time} de-eagering lever in the repository --- on record as ``a
weak knob, not recommended''. That verdict deserved a re-test, because the probe
behind it used a margin of zero at a dose worth \SIrange{1}{3}{\percent} of the
loss. This campaign asked whether the lever works when the margin is placed in the
measured \emph{right tail} of the transition-cosine distribution at
$\numrange{15}{60}\times$ that dose. The stake was deployability: at the time,
\texttt{mmr\_lambda} entered only through the evaluation path and never reached
serving, so the training-time regularizer was the one anti-eager candidate that
could actually ship.

\paragraph{The design was written so that a null would still be informative.} The
pre-registered primary was the paired $\Delta$ on \texttt{artist\_conc@10} against
an in-batch control, with holisticness@10 explicitly \emph{demoted} to secondary
because at the operating point $\partial H/\partial\texttt{music@10} =
+\num{0.2062}$ against $\partial H/\partial\texttt{eager} = -\num{0.0378}$ --- a
$\num{5.46}\times$ ratio that would have scored a \emph{successful} de-eagering as
approximately zero. A relevance floor was pre-registered in two parts: absolute
\texttt{music@10} $\ge\num{0.4300}$ \emph{and} a paired $\Delta$\texttt{music@10}
lower bound above $-\num{0.030}$. Eight \texttt{seq-dualgru} runs on the canonical
split \dsid{seq-20260715-131139}, all remote, \SI{60}{\minute} of compute, none
failed. The bit-identity gate passed at exactly \num{170} hits with the
reconstructed concentration matching the on-record arm to \num{15} decimal places,
and a penalty-active gate was pre-registered as a silent-failure detector: it
\textbf{passed on three arms and FAILED --- reporting the penalty inert --- on
four}, which is the campaign's result.

\emph{Results.} Table~\ref{tab:nexttrack-eagermargin} is the batch and
Table~\ref{tab:nexttrack-eagermargin-paired} the paired statistics.
Figure~\ref{fig:nexttrack-eager-margin} shows the mechanism.

  resamples, rng \num{1337}, $n=\num{1431}$). $n_{disc}$ counts sessions whose
  hit@10 changed. Measured half-widths on the \emph{active} arms
  (\numrange{0.0080}{0.0173}) match the design's predicted \num{0.0102}, so the
  instrument was correctly powered --- the axis failed anyway.
  \emph{Source: \texttt{2026-07-30-eager-margin-calibrated-refutation.md}.}}
  \label{tab:nexttrack-eagermargin-paired}
  {\footnotesize\setlength{\tabcolsep}{4.5pt}\begin{tabular}{lllrrr}
    \toprule
    Arm & $\Delta$\texttt{artist\_conc} [95\% CI] & $\Delta$\texttt{music@10} [95\% CI] & $\Delta H$ & $n_{disc}$ & mean $|\Delta$conc$|$ \\
    \midrule
    A1 & $-0.12987$ [$-0.14354$, $-0.11605$] & $-0.05535$ [$-0.06606$, $-0.04465$] & $-0.010445$ & 125 & 0.191304 \\
    A2 & $-0.23705$ [$-0.25493$, $-0.22026$] & $-0.09721$ [$-0.11107$, $-0.08333$] & $-0.020215$ & 166 & 0.282895 \\
    B1 & $-0.00298$ [$-0.01085$, $+0.00519$] & $-0.00796$ [$-0.01411$, $-0.00194$] & $-0.002183$ & 62 & 0.093734 \\
    B2 & $+0.00005$ [$-0.00028$, $+0.00039$] & $-0.00014$ [$-0.00037$, $+0.00000$] & $-0.000034$ & \best{0} & 0.000419 \\

    \bottomrule
  \end{tabular}}
\end{table}

\begin{figure}[ht]
  \centering
  \includegraphics[width=0.95\textwidth]{nexttrack_eager_margin}
  \caption{\textbf{Left:} the campaign's central result. The hinge's realized
  penalty at initialization, reconstructed from epoch-1 loss inflation at shared
  initialization (identical seed, so identical init and batch order). The margins
  were calibrated from the \emph{item}$\to$\emph{item} transition cosine (median
  \num{0.262}, p75 \num{0.584}, p90 \num{0.830}), but the penalty acts on
  \emph{prediction}$\to$\emph{current}, whose ceiling over training sits between
  \num{0.5843} and \num{0.70}: at $m\ge\num{0.5843}$ the penalized quantity never
  enters the hinge's support and the loss trace is bit-identical to the control at
  all \num{14} epochs, at $\beta$ up to the schema cap. \textbf{Right:} where the
  knob \emph{does} fire, eagerness and relevance are the same mechanism. Pushing
  the prediction off the current track's latent also pushes it off the region where
  the true next track lives, so there is no window between real de-eagering and
  breaching the relevance floor. The dash-dotted line marks the free on-record
  comparator that no arm here matched inside the floor.}
  \label{fig:nexttrack-eager-margin}
\end{figure}

\emph{Interpretation.} \textbf{The refutation is expressive, not dosimetric, and
that is the whole finding: the calibration measured the wrong distribution.} The
margins were drawn from $\cos(x_t, x_{t+1})$ --- how far apart two consecutively
played tracks are --- but the hinge acts on $\cos(\mathrm{pred}_t, x_t)$, how close
the model's \emph{prediction} sits to the current track. Those distributions are
not comparable. Reconstructing the hinge from epoch-1 loss inflation puts the
ceiling of the penalized quantity between \num{0.5843} and \num{0.70}, so p75 and
p90 of the real-transition distribution lie \emph{outside the support of the
quantity being penalized}, and arms B3 and B4 reproduced the control's loss to
\num{16} significant digits at every one of \num{14} epochs. The design's hedge ---
that the realized penalty would \emph{exceed} its nominal value --- is empirically
inverted by orders of magnitude. The reason is structural: an InfoNCE-trained
prediction is a \emph{hedged near-average} over many plausible continuations, so its
absolute cosine to any single item is bounded low. \textbf{Eagerness is a ranking
property --- whether the current track's neighbours occupy the top slots --- and a
hinge on an absolute cosine cannot express it.}

\textbf{Where the knob does fire, eagerness and relevance turn out to be the same
mechanism.} At $m=0$ the lever works spectacularly on the facet --- A2 cuts
concentration \num{0.371}$\to$\num{0.134} and drives \texttt{ild} to \num{0.676}
--- while \texttt{music@10} collapses to \num{0.358} and recall to \num{20} of
\num{1431}. There is no separating window: \SI{27}{\percent} of real transitions
are negative-cosine, so pushing the prediction away from the current track's latent
also pushes it out of the region where the true next track lives, and
\texttt{mood\_coh} collapses too (\num{0.480}$\to$\num{0.222}). Already at
$\beta=3$ the absolute relevance floor is breached. The pre-registered failure mode
fired exactly as written.

\textbf{The prior is sharpened rather than retired, and the usable band is
identified.} The 2026-07-22 probe at $\beta \le \num{0.2}$, $m=0$ reported ``weak''
--- correct in band, wrong in interpretation. That setting sits just \emph{under}
the destruction threshold, and by $\beta=3$ the same setting is a relevance
destroyer. The entire usable dynamic range at $m=0$ therefore lies between
$\beta=\num{0.2}$ and $\beta=\num{3}$, a factor of \num{15}, unexplored, bounded
above by degeneration and below by no effect. The dose ladder run here was
\textbf{not underpowered; it was aimed past the target}.

\textbf{Two second-order results worth keeping.} First, demoting $H$ to secondary
was load-bearing: A2 has by far the best facets and the \emph{worst} $H$
(\num{0.003569}), because the grounding factor annihilates it exactly as the
$\num{5.46}\times$ derivative ratio predicted. Had $H$ been primary this campaign
would have read as a flat null instead of a mapped frontier. Second, the cheapest
real de-eagering on record remains an \emph{architecture} setting, not this knob:
the free on-record comparator at \texttt{fusion\_layers}${}=1$ gives
$\Delta$concentration $-\num{0.051}$ at a $-\num{0.016}$ recall cost, and no arm
here matched that inside the relevance floor --- the floor-respecting arms delivered
between $-\num{0.003}$ and $+\num{0.0002}$, and the arms that beat $-\num{0.051}$
paid $\numrange{4}{8}\times$ the recall.

\paragraph{Why the approved later phases did not run.} All three were gated on a
phase-A win, and there is none. Phase C's arms were additionally defined \emph{by
reference} to the winner's margin and $\beta$, so with no winner they are
undefined; applied literally to the arms that passed the floors, the selection rule
would have spent three runs transferring a \emph{provably inert} setting to another
family. The runner stopped rather than improvise, which is the correct outcome: the
next move is a re-aimed design, not the approved phase C. Concretely, re-specify
the penalty rather than re-tune it --- hinge on a \emph{ranking} quantity such as
$\cos(\mathrm{pred}, x_t) - \cos(\mathrm{pred}, x_{t+1})$, or an explicit
same-artist mass term over the top-$k$ --- and, by elimination, make the eval-time
re-rank reach the serving path, since with the training-time lever refuted the only
lever that works was at that moment one the serving path could not reproduce. That
last observation is what the next section acts on.
\emph{Source: \texttt{2026-07-30-eager-margin-calibrated-refutation.md}.}

\section{Next-track: the hard per-artist cap, and the second evaluation surface}
\label{sec:nexttrack-artistcap}
\emph{Rationale.} Two \emph{soft} anti-eager levers had been refuted in the
preceding day. The training-time regularizer is mis-specified
(Section~\ref{sec:nexttrack-eagermargin}): it hinges on an absolute cosine that a
hedged InfoNCE prediction never drives above ${\sim}\num{0.6}$, while eagerness is
a \emph{ranking} property. The eval-time MMR re-rank is real
(Section~\ref{sec:nexttrack-mmr}) but measures \textbf{inert on the pathological
case}: on a real user playlist the model ranks \num{59} of its top \num{60}
candidates as one artist and the next artist first appears at \textbf{rank
\num{75}}, while MMR's entire diversity budget there is worth
$\num{0.3}\times\num{0.26}\approx\num{0.078}$ of normalized score --- nowhere near
enough to pay for a 70-rank relevance drop. Both failures share one mechanism:
\textbf{a soft penalty in score space loses to a steep relevance gradient.} The
lever tried here does not compete on score at all. A \emph{hard} per-artist cap on
the top-$k$ \textbf{cannot be outscored} --- the same property the lab's journey
cooldown already relied on --- and it is the first de-eagering lever in this project
that works.

\paragraph{The second evaluation surface, in brief.}
\label{sec:nexttrack-walksurface}
Every next-track number in the sections above is a \emph{one-shot} top-10: score
the catalog once from a held-out session prefix and look for the next distinct
track. The shipped showcase, however, serves an \emph{autoregressive walk} --- it
appends its own prediction to the prefix and steps again, twenty times --- and a run
of individually legal steps can end in a completely different region of the latent
space while every single step looks fine. Drift is in fact invisible \emph{by
construction} on the one-shot surface, because the \texttt{mood\_coh} facet of
holisticness@10 measures each candidate's cosine to the \emph{prefix} centroid,
which on a walk travels with the model. A separate measurement line --- consolidated
in full in the companion next-track document and grounded in the user's own
\num{73632} consecutive real transitions, whose median step cosine is \num{0.261}
with \SI{27.0}{\percent} of steps \emph{negative} --- therefore added four
walk-level metrics: \texttt{vibe} (mean cosine of each generated track to the
\emph{fixed seed} centroid: does the journey hold its mood), \texttt{stride\_err}
($|$median step cosine $-\,\num{0.261}|$, lower better: does it move like the
listener it imitates), \texttt{drift} (end-of-journey displacement, nearer zero
better), \texttt{genres} (distinct genres visited, counted as a \emph{credit}) and
\texttt{ground} (a degeneracy floor: graded agreement with the real continuation).
Two deterministic retrieval-time controls tune the walk without touching the model
--- a seed \emph{anchor} $a$ and a \emph{stride} term $s$, one pulling toward a fixed
point and one pushing off the last point --- so a walk configuration is written as a
cell $a$/$s$. The deployed engine is a dual-tower GRU reading \texttt{latent} and
\texttt{cummean} views with a purely linear fusion, at cell
$a\num{0.8}$/$s\num{0.5}$; it is this corpus's only measured architecture
\emph{win}, and it won on the walk surface after three campaigns had refuted its
family on recall.

\paragraph{Implementation, and why it ports where MMR cannot.} The cap is applied
to the \emph{ordering}, after MMR, in both the training-time evaluation and the
serving ranker, so evaluation and serving agree by construction --- which avoids the
divergence class fixed earlier the same day. It is threaded through all five
declaring predictor families with a default of ``off'', so every pre-existing run
and model is byte-identical. It is \textbf{best-effort rather than absolute}
(unit-verified): the scan walks the whole order looking for admissible artists, but
where a prefix's candidate space is exhausted the remaining slots are backfilled
with deferred items instead of returning fewer than $k$; identity holds when the cap
is off or $\ge k$, and unknown-artist items are never capped. Crucially it needs
\emph{no sonic vectors}, so unlike MMR it ports to a thin client unchanged --- MMR
would require shipping a \SI{48}{\mega\byte} similarity dial against the
${\sim}\SI{6}{\mega\byte}$ one already baked.

\emph{Results.} Table~\ref{tab:nexttrack-artistcap} is the cap on the one-shot
surface, measured off-run: the scores are computed once per model and memoized, so
every cap arm sees \emph{bit-identical} score vectors and the pairing is exact
rather than merely matched. Figure~\ref{fig:nexttrack-artist-cap} plots the
trade-off.

\begin{table}[ht]
  \centering
  \caption{The hard per-artist cap on the one-shot surface, canonical split
  \dsid{seq-20260715-131139} ($n=\num{1431}$), on the deployed champion and on the
  champion plus the evidence gate of Section~\ref{sec:nexttrack-markovgate}. Paired
  on bit-identical cached scores (\num{2000} resamples, rng \num{1337}). H@10 is
  generation~3 (Section~\ref{sec:nexttrack-power-generations}). R@10, MRR, mus@10,

\begin{figure}[ht]
  \centering
  \includegraphics[width=0.95\textwidth]{nexttrack_artist_cap}
  \caption{\textbf{Left:} artist concentration collapses,
  \num{0.692}$\to$\num{0.113} at cap 3 on the gated champion. \textbf{Middle:} the
  result that was \emph{not} predicted --- graded relevance \emph{rises}, with the
  paired CI above zero at every cap on both models, and it replicates on the
  disjoint \SI{64}{\percent}-cold split at $+\num{0.026}$. The pre-registered worry
  was that a cap would de-eager by degrading into generic filler; the opposite
  happens, because the items promoted from rank \num{75} and beyond are \emph{more}
  musically apt to the true continuation than the same-artist tracks they displace.
  \textbf{Right:} the price, on the recall $\times$ crown plane. The cap is
  \emph{priced}, not free: gate $+$ cap 3 costs \num{52} of \num{1431} exact hits
  against the deployed status quo, while landing a holisticness@10 about
  \SI{40}{\percent} above the incumbent crown at $\num{1.5}\times$ its recall. Every
  arm clears the \num{155}-hit recall floor, so none is an instance of the
  ``maximize holisticness by predicting worse'' pathology.}
  \label{fig:nexttrack-artist-cap}
\end{figure}

\emph{Interpretation.} \textbf{\texttt{music@10} goes up, with the CI above zero, at
every cap on both models} ($+\num{0.0105}$ to $+\num{0.0230}$). This is the
campaign's most important result and it was not predicted: \textbf{the cap trades
\emph{literal} relevance for \emph{graded} relevance}. Only exact Recall@10
regresses. At gate $+$ cap 5 against the deployed status quo, recall falls
\num{0.2117}$\to$\num{0.1985} ($-\num{19}$ hits) while MRR \emph{rises}
\num{0.1220}$\to$\num{0.1311}, concentration falls \num{0.692}$\to$\num{0.231},
\texttt{ild} rises \num{0.406}$\to$\num{0.580}, \texttt{mood\_coh} rises
\num{0.398}$\to$\num{0.414}, \texttt{music@10} rises \num{0.454}$\to$\num{0.506},
and holisticness@10 rises \num{0.0086}$\to$\num{0.0401}.

\textbf{The cap does not pay for itself out of the gate's gain, and that must be
stated plainly.} The gate is $+\num{29}$ hits; the mildest cap costs $-\num{48}$ on
the gated model. So gate $+$ cap is net $\mathbf{-52}$ hits against the deployed
status quo at cap 3. Anyone who cares about exact recall should read this as a
trade-off, not a free win; the project's stated objective is session holisticness,
where it is a $\num{5.6}\times$ gain. Every cap arm clears the \num{0.108} recall
floor (\numrange{0.151}{0.198}), so these are not instances of the pathology the
floor exists to reject.

\textbf{The cap binds only where there is a problem.} On an already-varied top-10
it is a literal no-op at every cap value. The spot-check on three real user
playlists makes this concrete, counting distinct artists in the top-10: on a
playlist the champion already serves with 8 distinct artists, the cap changes
\emph{nothing} at any value. On the pathological playlist --- the case that defeated
both soft levers outright --- it goes from \num{1} distinct artist to \num{5} at cap
3, i.e.\ from ten consecutive tracks by one artist to a genuine spread across five.
And it \textbf{exactly repairs the gate's only regression}: the gate alone takes one
playlist from 8 distinct artists to 4 (concentration \num{0.044}$\to$\num{0.467}),
and at cap 3 it is back to 7 (\num{0.089}), at cap 2 to 8 (\num{0.044}) ---
identical to the deployed champion. The gate's accuracy gain therefore becomes
available \emph{without} its diversity cost.

\paragraph{Confirmed by registered runs, then hardened on a second split.} Three
registered runs on the canonical split, launched from the gated champion's
registry-declared hyperparameters with only the cap varying, reproduced
\textbf{every reported figure to every digit}
(\runid{run-20260730-235524-0ec1d-seq-blend} at cap off,
\runid{run-20260730-235524-93d38-seq-blend} at cap 3,
\runid{run-20260730-234635-a6252-seq-blend} at cap 5), so the off-run measurement
transfers exactly and the plumbing is verified end to end. Four further runs on the
disjoint \SI{64}{\percent}-cold split \dsid{seq-20260718-211238} then tested two
pre-registered legs, \emph{both} required: $\Delta$concentration
$\le-\num{0.05}$ with the CI below zero, \emph{and} a $\Delta$\texttt{music@10}
lower bound \emph{above} zero --- graded relevance had to keep \emph{improving},
not merely avoid collapse. \textbf{Both hold} at cap 3 (concentration
$-\num{0.52699}$ [$-\num{0.54546}$, $-\num{0.50825}$]; \texttt{music@10}
$+\num{0.02644}$ [$+\num{0.01919}$, $+\num{0.03368}$]), with the recall cost
\emph{smaller} and the relevance gain \emph{larger} on the colder window, and with
absolute concentration at cap 3 within \num{0.008} of the canonical figure. The
same batch delivered a third independent reproduction of the evidence gate's paired
effect, matching the recorded $+\num{0.01957}$ exactly.

\paragraph{Three cautions, one of them a lesson about floors.} First, the canonical
split's \emph{absolute} \texttt{music@10} $\ge\num{0.43}$ floor does \textbf{not}
transfer: that split's own control sits at \num{0.4295} on a legitimately colder
window, so pre-registering the rule on the paired $\Delta$ rather than an absolute
threshold is the only reason this did not read as a false failure. \textbf{Derive
floors per baseline.} Second, the measurement is at $k=\num{10}$ only, and $k$
interacts with the cap: a \emph{fixed} cap 3 at $k=\num{50}$ drives concentration
to \num{0.021} --- five times past the hardened operating point --- and reaches base
rank p95 \num{278}, whereas scaling the cap to preserve the \emph{share} reaches
only p95 \num{156} and holds concentration at \num{0.118}. Scaled is both gentler
on relevance and faithful to what was validated, and the two are identical at
$k=\num{10}$; it is implemented as \SI{30}{\percent} of slots with a floor of 3, in
both the client and the server ranker, so the two paths agree by construction.
Third, nothing here was promoted, registered as a champion or deployed, and the
recommendation on record is \texttt{markov\_gate} with cap 3 as the next deployment
candidate (cap 5 as the conservative alternative, whose MRR is still above today's).
An open eval-versus-serving row-level divergence, documented separately, means
production output will be close to rather than identical with these numbers.

\paragraph{Two engineering findings that generalize.} First, a bit-identity gate
caught an \emph{invalid launch}: the initial control ran against a stale remote
checkout containing zero occurrences of either new flag, so both hyperparameters
were silently ignored and the run reported the \emph{ungated, uncapped} champion's
metrics under gated hyperparameters --- a wrong number, not a crash. The lesson is
sharper than ``resync the worker'': \textbf{checksum the predictor files for the
family being launched immediately before posting}, not at design time, because the
preceding campaign had verified two \emph{other} families' files. Second, the same
incident left an orphaned run row stuck at \texttt{running}, which neither the stop
nor the delete endpoint can clear (the former tracks only local runs, the latter
refuses runs claimed by a worker) --- a gap worth closing before it happens again.
Both levers were subsequently ported to the two showcase clients as pure
post-processing, with no model rebuild, no new vectors and no retraining, and with
parity tests pinning the ported ordering against the Python reference; in one client
the champion engine turns out to be \textbf{degenerate uncapped} --- thirty tracks by
one artist on an Argentine-rock seed --- so there the cap is load-bearing rather
than cosmetic.
\emph{Source: \texttt{2026-07-30c-artist-cap-measurement.md}.}

\section{Next-track: stage-stack topology: depth refuted on both surfaces}
\label{sec:nexttrack-stagestack}
\emph{Rationale.} The user's question was direct: does an explicit \emph{stack} of
per-step MLP and recurrent stages beat a single GRU, on a corpus where five
consecutive architecture campaigns have returned nulls? Four arrangements were
specified --- \texttt{ann+gru+ann+gru}, \texttt{ann+gru+gru+ann},
\texttt{ann+gru+gru+gru}, \texttt{gru+gru+gru+gru} --- plus a fifth,
\texttt{gru+ann+gru+gru}, chosen by the user to test \emph{why} a leading MLP hurt
at $N=1$: Section~\ref{sec:nexttrack-preencoder} attributed that loss to half-wave
rectification destroying signed prefix information that a GRU's gated linear input
transform preserves, so putting the MLP on a \emph{gated hidden state} instead of
raw signed PCA should make the harm vanish. This is also the first campaign in this
arc to be judged on \emph{two} surfaces by design rather than in retrospect, and it
sets the standard the campaign after it inherits.

\paragraph{Design, and the equivalence proof that made a control possible.} Seven
runs of a new predictor, \texttt{seq-stack}, on the canonical split
\dsid{seq-20260715-131139} (\num{1431} test sessions), \SI{90}{\minute} of compute,
zero failures, zero contingency runs spent. Capacity was equalised at
${\sim}\num{1282000}$ parameters across the five 4-stage arms (mutual spread
\SI{0.445}{\percent}), and all seven counts were re-verified by instantiating the
model rather than trusted from the design. Everything but the stage string and the
widths is held: dropout \num{0.1}, InfoNCE, $\tau$~\num{0.07}, \num{40} epochs,
patience \num{6}, lr \num{0.001}, batch \num{128}, seed \num{1337}, MMR off, artist
cap off.

Bit-identity with the family's historical \num{176} hits is \emph{not available}
here and its absence is not a failure: the trainer re-seeds on entry, so handing it
a pre-built model offsets the dropout stream even when the initial weights match.
The campaign therefore gated on a \textbf{functional-equivalence proof} plus an
\textbf{in-batch control}, and every verdict is stated against that control. The
proof was run \emph{before} the registry edit and the server restart, so a failure
would have cost nothing: at \texttt{stages=["gru"]} the new model computes the same
function as the incumbent single GRU, with $\max|\Delta|$ \textbf{\texttt{0.00e+00}}
on both \texttt{forward} and \texttt{predict\_next} at prefix lengths
$T=1,2,5,17,40$ and \num{394944} parameters on both sides. The in-batch control
then returned \textbf{\num{178} of \num{1431}}, inside the pre-registered band
$[\num{155},\num{198}]$ and only \num{2} hits off the historical \num{176} ---
exactly the small offset the re-seeding argument predicts. Two further gates
mattered: metric completeness was green on all seven runs (no worker-image key
stripping of the kind Section~\ref{sec:nexttrack-mmr} documents), and the walk instrument
was verified against the shipped engine, whose $\Delta$\texttt{vibe}
($-\num{0.0033}$) and $\Delta$\texttt{stride\_err} ($+\num{0.0137}$) both straddle
zero against the freshly-trained control. The walk harness additionally reproduced
\emph{all ten} previously recorded means exactly and stayed bit-stable as arms were
added, which is what licenses pairing across separate invocations.

\emph{Results, the one-shot surface.} Table~\ref{tab:nexttrack-stagestack-s1}.

\begin{table}[ht]
  \centering
  \caption{The stage-stack batch on the one-shot surface, seven runs on
  \dsid{seq-20260715-131139} ($n_{\mathrm{test}}=\num{1431}$), sorted by
  Recall@10. Run ids are \protect\runid{run-20260801-} plus the chunk time and hex
  suffix, in full: \texttt{003536-eaf81} (C0), \texttt{003536-bcedf} (C1),
  \texttt{003646-07994} (B), \texttt{003646-8cc2d} (E), \texttt{003646-dbe68} (C),
  \texttt{003536-d612e} (D), \texttt{003646-6e3bf} (A). Stage codes: \texttt{a}
  $=$ per-step MLP, \texttt{g} $=$ GRU. MRR is truncated at \num{10} and

  exactly. The paired $\Delta$ is against the in-batch control C0 (\num{2000}
  resamples, rng \num{1337}). Truncated MRR@10 and \texttt{mood\_coh} are omitted
  for width and track recall closely: MRR@10 \num{0.04555} (C0), \num{0.04579}
  (C1), then \numrange{0.02080}{0.02894} across the five 4-stage arms;
  \texttt{mood\_coh} \num{0.4788} (C0), \num{0.4856} (C1), then
  \numrange{0.3821}{0.3964}. R@10, H@10, mus@10
  higher-better; \texttt{conc}, \texttt{adj} lower-better --- and the
  lowest-eagerness cells belong to \emph{refuted} arms, which is what the recall
  floor is for. \texttt{ild} rises monotonically as the arms get worse
  (\num{0.4549} on C0, \num{0.4424} on C1, then \numrange{0.5112}{0.5373} across
  the five 4-stage arms) and is omitted here for width.
  \emph{Source: \texttt{2026-08-01-stage-stack-topology.md}.}}
  \label{tab:nexttrack-stagestack-s1}

    C1 \emph{cap.} & \texttt{bcedf} & \texttt{gru} h537 & \num{1280937} & 0.12159 (174) & $-0.00280$ [$-.0119$, $+.0056$] & 0.023418 & 0.3773 & 0.3653 & 0.4556 \\

No arm meets any win clause. Every 4-stage arm loses recall \emph{and}
holisticness by paired CI, and all five sit \textbf{below the mandatory
\num{0.108} absolute recall floor} (\numrange{0.0706}{0.0860}). Derived
per-baseline from the control's own \texttt{music@10} of \num{0.45519}, every
4-stage arm is \SIrange{5.0}{8.4}{\percent} below the relevance floor too, so none
is even relevance-neutral. The losses are $\numrange{2.5}{3.7}\times$ the measured
half-width: this is not a noise-band question.

\emph{Results, the walk surface.} Table~\ref{tab:nexttrack-stagestack-s2}, at the matched
primary cell $a\num{0.4}$/$s\num{0.3}$, paired against the in-batch control on the
walk metrics of Section~\ref{sec:nexttrack-walksurface}. \texttt{vibe} is always reported with
\texttt{stride\_err} beside it, at matched stride, per the standing caveat that a
vibe gain bought by taking shorter steps is not a gain.
