Every 4-stage arm fires the refuted clause. D fires it \emph{twice}, also failing
the grounding floor ($\Delta$\texttt{ground} $-\num{0.0250}$ [$-\num{0.0474}$,
$-\num{0.0048}$]), and A additionally loses genre variety ($-\num{1.200}$
[$-\num{2.125}$, $-\num{0.300}$]). No arm approaches a win clause. Two robustness
checks close the escape routes. A pre-registered \textbf{stride probe} over
$s\in\{\num{0.2},\num{0.3},\num{0.4}\}$ was run for the only two arms with any
directional movement toward a walk win, and \emph{both peak at $s\num{0.3}$ --- the
cell they were already measured in} (C: vibe/stride\_err \num{0.586}/\num{0.183},
\num{0.548}/\num{0.113}, \num{0.517}/\num{0.153}; E: \num{0.576}/\num{0.155},
\num{0.540}/\num{0.109}, \num{0.516}/\num{0.174}); at $s\num{0.4}$ both lose vibe
with the CI below zero while \texttt{stride\_err} gets \emph{worse}. And the single
best arm was re-measured at the \textbf{deployed} cell $a\num{0.8}$/$s\num{0.5}$,
where it is refuted again ($\Delta$\texttt{vibe} $-\num{0.0158}$
[$-\num{0.0267}$, $-\num{0.0059}$], $\Delta$\texttt{stride\_err} straddling).

\begin{figure}[ht]
  \centering
  \includegraphics[width=0.95\textwidth]{nexttrack_stage_stack}
  \caption{\textbf{Left:} all five 4-stage arms fall \emph{through} the mandatory
  absolute recall floor, while the $\num{3.24}\times$ capacity control ties the
  anchor --- so the damage is depth, not capacity. Error bars are the paired
  bootstrap CI against the in-batch control, mapped onto the hit scale.
  \textbf{Middle:} the campaign's most interesting number. Arm B reaches the
  \emph{best} validation InfoNCE in the batch and retrieves \num{55} fewer hits
  than the control: the objective the whole family trains on and top-10 retrieval
  quality come apart at depth, so any architecture search selecting on validation
  loss would pick the wrong arm. \textbf{Right:} the two-surface closure. The walk
  read at the matched primary cell refutes the same five arms that the one-shot
  surface refutes; the capacity control ties on both. The axis does not merely fail
  to close on one surface --- it closes on two.}
  \label{fig:nexttrack-stage-stack}
\end{figure}

\emph{Interpretation.} \textbf{The axis is closed, and it is the first null in this
arc to close on both surfaces.} All five 4-stage arms are refuted by paired CI on
one-shot recall \emph{and} on the walk with the instrument gate green, and the
capacity control is indistinguishable on both. This is the sixth consecutive
architecture null on this corpus and, unlike the five before it, it satisfies the
standing rule that a one-shot verdict says nothing about the surface the product
serves.

\textbf{The user's mechanism hypothesis is refuted, and that is the most reusable
fact here.} Arm E put the MLP on a \emph{gated hidden state} rather than on raw
signed PCA, which the rectifier story predicts should remove the harm. It does not:
E reaches \num{118} of \num{1431}, ties two other arrangements, beats only the
worst, and comes nowhere near the control's \num{178}. So half-wave rectification
of the \emph{raw input} is not the mechanism --- whatever is wrong survives moving
the rectifier downstream of a gate. One run bought a clean refutation of a
plausible and cheap-to-believe story.

\textbf{The damage is depth itself, and three controls close the alternatives
simultaneously.} It is not capacity: the $\num{3.24}\times$ control ties on both
surfaces. It is not regularization or early stopping: \emph{every} 4-stage arm
trains \emph{longer} than the control (\numrange{21}{40} epochs against \num{14}),
and two of five reach a \emph{lower} best validation loss, so the pre-authorized
dropout-count contingency failed both of its trigger clauses and was not spent ---
the four-fold dropout application is exonerated. And it is not the MLP: a leading
per-step MLP at depth 4 is recall-neutral and \emph{holisticness-positive} against
the pure-recurrent arrangement ($\Delta H$ $+\num{0.00291}$, CI above zero). What
remains is the depth of the recurrent chain, and its cost is large ---
\numrange{55}{77} hits of \num{1431}.

\textbf{Bracketing beats interleaving at byte-identical capacity.}
\texttt{ann+gru+gru+ann} beats \texttt{ann+gru+ann+gru} by $+\num{0.01537}$ recall
with the CI above zero at \emph{the same parameter count by formula} --- the
tightest-controlled topology comparison the campaign could make. Interposing a
per-step MLP \emph{between} two recurrences is worse than putting MLPs on the
outside, which is consistent with the depth reading: breaking the recurrent chain
in the middle costs more than adding depth at the ends. Two further contrasts are
flat: the number of recurrent stages (2 versus 3) straddles on recall, and the
rectifier's position buys nothing beyond beating the worst arrangement.

\textbf{A leading per-step MLP is \emph{not} harmful at depth 4, which narrows
rather than confirms the $N=1$ result.} Section~\ref{sec:nexttrack-preencoder}'s ``leading
MLP is harmful'' was measured at $N=1$ against a single GRU; at depth 4 the same
addition is free-to-slightly-good. The pre-encoder pitfall should be scoped to the
shallow case.

\textbf{Objective and retrieval dissociate at depth, and this is the sharpest
instance yet.} Arm B achieves the \emph{lowest} validation InfoNCE in the entire
batch (\num{6.5378} against the control's \num{6.5832}) while retrieving
\textbf{\num{55} fewer hits} (\num{123} against \num{178}). Every prior
architecture null on this corpus was consistent with ``the extra machinery does
nothing''; this one shows the extra machinery does something measurable to the loss
and that it is the \emph{wrong} something. The operational consequence is blunt:
select on Recall@10 and the walk, never on the training objective. One caveat rides
with it --- B ran to the \num{40}-epoch cap with patience never triggering, so its
recall is a \emph{lower bound}, and a longer re-run is the clean test of the
dissociation (the prediction being a better validation loss and no better recall).

\textbf{The eagerness facets improve monotonically as the arms get worse, and the
floors caught it.} Every 4-stage arm de-eagers substantially (concentration
\num{0.359}$\to$\numrange{0.263}{0.318}) and raises \texttt{ild}; under a naive
holisticness-primary read some would look interesting. All are killed by the
absolute recall floor and by the per-baseline relevance floor --- the same mechanism
that has now saved four campaigns. Note that the canonical \emph{absolute}
\num{0.43} relevance figure would have passed several of these arms, so the floor
must be derived per baseline.

\paragraph{Two by-products, and the governance line.} A previously closed $N=2$
relative --- a dual tower with a pre-MLP on one side, closed on a one-shot recall
tie --- was walk-evaluated for the first time here and is \textbf{refuted on the
walk} ($\Delta$\texttt{vibe} $-\num{0.0255}$ [$-\num{0.0432}$, $-\num{0.0096}$]),
its one real gain being less drift. That is the same signature a recurrent-cell
variant showed earlier --- better stride and drift, worse vibe --- which now looks
like a general property of adding pre-recurrence machinery rather than anything
specific to either arm. Second, the grounding metric again discriminated almost
nothing, separating one arm of nine, consistent with its recorded arithmetic
(between-arm range \num{0.0060} against a per-session standard deviation of
\num{0.1417}, needing ${\sim}\num{2140}$ sessions): it is a floor that catches
degeneracy, not a ranking metric, and it was used only that way. Nothing was
promoted as champion, nothing was registered as a crown claim and nothing was
deployed; the seven arms were promoted \emph{for measurement only}, each carrying
the verbatim note ``promoted to enable walk evaluation; not a crown claim'', which
flooded the best-models group and was handed to the curation agent.

\paragraph{Two process findings worth keeping.} First, the predictor-checksum gate
fired \emph{on a file the design did not name for copying} --- it listed one module
to copy and a shared module only to \emph{verify}, and the verification failed. The
divergence turned out to be inert for this batch, but that is a \emph{post-hoc}
judgement that required reading the diff; the rule that caught it is the one worth
keeping. \textbf{Verify the checksum of every file in the family's import closure,
not just the ones you copied.} Second, the approved launch body was rejected by the
approved registry block: the design's JSON sent six hyperparameters the block does
not declare, so the first posts failed outright. It was resolved by dropping the
six keys \emph{after proving the drop is a no-op} --- the predictor's defaults supply
exactly those values and the resolved hyperparameter dict is identical either way,
verified by direct comparison --- and the registry block was left exactly as
approved.
\emph{Source: \texttt{2026-08-01-stage-stack-topology.md}.}

\section{Next-track: parallel tower banks closed on both surfaces}
\label{sec:nexttrack-towerbank}
This is the seventh architecture campaign on the next-track corpus and the one
that closes the \emph{parallel} branch of the axis. It is the second campaign
consolidated here judged on \textbf{both} evaluation surfaces, to the standard
Section~\ref{sec:nexttrack-stagestack} set, so it needs one paragraph of
orientation before the results.

\paragraph{The second surface, in brief.} Every next-track number in the
sections above is a \emph{one-shot} top-10: score the catalog once from a session
prefix and look for the held-out next track. The shipped showcase, however, serves
an \emph{autoregressive walk} --- it appends its own prediction to the prefix and
steps again, twenty times --- and a run of individually legal steps can end in a
completely different region of the latent space while every single step looks
fine. A separate measurement line (introduced in
Section~\ref{sec:nexttrack-walksurface} and consolidated in full in the companion
next-track document, and grounded in the user's own \num{73632} consecutive real
transitions, whose median step cosine is \num{0.261}) therefore added four
walk-level metrics: \texttt{vibe} (mean cosine of each generated track to the
\emph{seed} centroid --- does the journey hold its mood), \texttt{stride\_err}
($|$median step cosine $-\,\num{0.261}|$, lower better --- does it move like the
listener it imitates), \texttt{drift} (end-of-journey displacement, nearer zero
better), \texttt{genres} (distinct genres visited) and \texttt{ground} (a
degeneracy floor: agreement with the real continuation). Two deterministic
retrieval-time controls tune the walk without touching the model --- a seed
\emph{anchor} $a$ and a \emph{stride} term $s$ --- so a walk configuration is
written as a cell $a$/$s$. The deployed engine is a dual-tower GRU reading
\texttt{latent} and \texttt{cummean} views with a purely linear fusion
(\texttt{fusion\_layers}${}=0$) at cell $a\num{0.8}$/$s\num{0.5}$; it is this
corpus's only measured architecture \emph{win}, and it won on the walk surface
after three campaigns had refuted its family on recall.

\emph{Rationale.} That win came with a stated mechanism: the tower carries a
\textbf{fixed reference never overwritten by recurrence}, which is what holds a
mood. A mechanism of that shape makes a testable prediction --- it should be
\emph{dose-responsive}. If one fixed-reference tower buys a held vibe plus
$+\num{1.5}$ genres, then $N$ towers, enough of them on the raw \texttt{latent}
view to hold the recall floor and the rest on \texttt{cummean}, should buy
\emph{more} of the same. This campaign runs that dose curve.

The vehicle is a new predictor, \texttt{seq-bank}: $N$ \emph{parallel}
single-layer bare GRU towers over configurable causal views, their per-step hidden
states concatenated and read by \textbf{one linear head}, with tower count and
view multiset collapsed into a \emph{single} \texttt{views} hyperparameter so that
$N$ cannot drift out of sync with the views. What it cannot express matters as
much as what it can: it declares exactly \num{15} hyperparameters, and none of
\texttt{fusion\_layers}, \texttt{fusion\_hidden}, \texttt{pre\_hidden},
\texttt{pre\_layers}, \texttt{layers}, \texttt{num\_layers}, \texttt{arch},
\texttt{bidirectional}, \texttt{residual} or \texttt{eager\_beta} is among them.
Every mechanism the sections above already refuted --- the MLP fusion head
(Section~\ref{sec:nexttrack-dualtower}), the per-step pre-encoder
(Section~\ref{sec:nexttrack-preencoder}), the soft anti-eager regularizer
(Section~\ref{sec:nexttrack-holisticness}) --- is therefore \emph{structurally
unreachable} rather than merely unused, and a \texttt{parse\_views} guard rejects
any spec without a \texttt{latent} tower, putting the registered
\numrange{0.058}{0.087} \texttt{delta}/\texttt{cummean} pathologies out of reach
as well. Two hypotheses were pre-registered: the \emph{dose} hypothesis above,
and the independent sub-hypothesis that if the raw \texttt{latent} view
\emph{subsumes} its siblings (the dual-tower campaign's mechanism), then $N$
identical \texttt{latent} towers buy nothing at any $N$.

\paragraph{Design.} Ten runs on the canonical split
\dsid{seq-20260715-131139} (\num{1431} test sessions, cold-item rate
\num{0.5507}), \SI{84.2}{\minute} of compute, zero failures. Everything but
\texttt{views} / \texttt{hidden} / \texttt{dropout} is held: \texttt{loss}
InfoNCE, $\tau=\num{0.07}$, \num{40} epochs, patience \num{6}, lr \num{0.001},
batch \num{128}, seed \num{1337}, MMR off, artist cap off. \textbf{Capacity is
matched at \num{3.00}$\times$ the h256 baseline} for every bank arm \emph{and}
for the single-GRU control C1 (h512), with counts re-verified from the artifacts
rather than trusted from the design --- \texttt{seq\_bank} emits
\texttt{n\_params} into \texttt{metrics.json}, and the mutual spread across the
seven \num{3.00}$\times$ arms including C1 is \SI{0.405}{\percent}. The decision
rule was written on both surfaces before launch. On \textbf{S1} (one-shot
recall@10) a win requires a paired $\Delta$ above zero against \emph{both} the
h256 anchor C0 \emph{and} the parameter-matched C1, subject to an absolute recall
floor ($\ge\num{0.108}$, i.e.\ \num{155} of \num{1431}) and a per-baseline
relevance floor (paired $\Delta$\,music@10 vs C0 must not fall below zero). On
\textbf{S2} (the walk: \num{40} held-out sessions $\times$ \num{20} steps at
artist cap 1, paired against the \emph{in-batch} C0) the primary cell is
$a\num{0.4}$/$s\num{0.3}$, and a saved definition additionally requires
\textbf{the same sign at the deployed cell} $a\num{0.8}$/$s\num{0.5}$.

\paragraph{The instrument was pinned before anything was measured.} All ten
pre-registered gates came back green, and three are worth recording because they
make the comparison unusually tight. A \emph{functional-equivalence} gate showed
\texttt{seq-bank} to be a strict generalization of both incumbents rather than a
new family: at \texttt{views="latent"} it reproduces the single-GRU
\texttt{SeqNextLatent} and at \texttt{views="latent+latent"} the
\texttt{fusion\_layers}${}=0$ \texttt{DualTowerNextLatent}, with $\max|\Delta|$
\textbf{\texttt{0.00e+00}} on both \texttt{forward} and \texttt{predict\_next} at
prefix lengths $T=1,2,5,17,40$ and identical parameter counts on both sides; an
extra training-stream check matched per-epoch train \emph{and} validation loss to
the last digit over three epochs, with all ten state-dict tensors at
$\max|\Delta|$ \texttt{0.00e+00}. Both bit-identity gates then hit exactly: C0
reproduced its on-record recall@10 \num{0.12299091544374564} $=$ \num{176} of
\num{1431} --- a \textbf{10th} reproduction, with \texttt{music@10}, H@10 and MRR
matching to the last digit --- and R2 reproduced the dual-tower
\texttt{latent/latent} f0 arm at \num{0.1187980433263452} $=$ \num{170} of
\num{1431}, so the design's band fallback was never needed. The walk instrument
was checked against the shipped \texttt{gru.onnx}: $\Delta$\texttt{vibe}
$+\num{0.0028}$ and $\Delta$\texttt{stride\_err} $+\num{0.0005}$ both straddle
zero, so S2 verdicts against the in-batch C0 are not an artefact of the base.

\emph{Results, S1.} Table~\ref{tab:nexttrack-towerbank-s1} is the batch.
\textbf{No arm is bolded as a winner because there is none}: no arm achieves even
the first leg of the win rule, all nine non-anchor arms are ties against C0, and
\emph{five of the eight bank arms lose to the parameter-matched single GRU C1 with
the CI below zero}. Both floors pass for every arm --- the worst is V1 at
\num{162} hits, clear of the \num{155} floor, and the most negative relevance
$\Delta$ is V2's $-\num{0.006109}$, straddling.

    C1 \emph{ctrl} & \texttt{91e31} & 1$\times$GRU h512 & \num{1182912} & \best{0.12998 (186)} & $+0.0070$ [$-.002$, $+.016$] & --- & 0.02380 & 0.4581 & 0.3825 \\
    B4 & \texttt{1d662} & lat$\times$4 h211 & \num{1187700} & \best{0.12998 (186)} & $+0.0070$ [$-.002$, $+.017$] & $+0.0000$ & 0.02446 & \best{0.4594} & 0.3716 \\
    D2 & \texttt{62604} & lat$\times$2 h256 d\num{0.1} & \num{789696} & 0.12369 (177) & $+0.0007$ [$-.008$, $+.008$] & $-0.0063$ & 0.02426 & 0.4567 & 0.3698 \\
    C0 \emph{base} & \texttt{91b54} & 1$\times$GRU h256 & \num{394944} & 0.12299 (176) & --- & $-0.0070$ & \best{0.02454} & 0.4558 & 0.3559 \\
    B3 & \texttt{90866} & lat$\times$3 h256 & \num{1184448} & 0.12020 (172) & $-0.0028$ [$-.010$, $+.006$] & $\mathbf{-0.0098}$\textsuperscript{$\ast$} & 0.02358 & 0.4554 & 0.3764 \\
    R2 & \texttt{2e6f2} & lat$\times$2 h256 d\num{0.0} & \num{789696} & 0.11880 (170) & $-0.0042$ [$-.013$, $+.004$] & $\mathbf{-0.0112}$\textsuperscript{$\ast$} & 0.02378 & 0.4554 & 0.3706 \\
    B2 & \texttt{67185} & lat$\times$2 h334 & \num{1186560} & 0.11880 (170) & $-0.0042$ [$-.013$, $+.003$] & $\mathbf{-0.0112}$\textsuperscript{$\ast$} & 0.02336 & 0.4554 & 0.3799 \\
    V3 & \texttt{17502} & lat$+$cum$+$cum h256 & \num{1184448} & 0.11880 (170) & $-0.0042$ [$-.016$, $+.006$] & $-0.0112$ & 0.02320 & 0.4497 & 0.3581 \\
    V2 & \texttt{836b1} & lat$+$cum$+$del h256 & \num{1184448} & 0.11600 (166) & $-0.0070$ [$-.020$, $+.005$] & $\mathbf{-0.0140}$\textsuperscript{$\ast$} & 0.02245 & 0.4497 & 0.3547 \\
    V1 & \texttt{b5928} & lat$\times$2$+$cum h256 & \num{1184448} & 0.11321 (162) & $-0.0098$ [$-.022$, $+.001$] & $\mathbf{-0.0168}$\textsuperscript{$\ast$} & 0.02299 & 0.4500 & 0.3572 \\
    \bottomrule
  \end{tabular}}
\end{table}

\paragraph{The sharpest number in the batch is a zero.} B4 --- four independent
h211 \texttt{latent} towers, \num{1187700} parameters --- and C1 --- one h512
GRU, \num{1182912} parameters --- return the \textbf{identical \num{186} hits of
\num{1431}}, a paired $\Delta$ of precisely $\num{0.000000}$
$[-\num{0.00839},+\num{0.00839}]$. At a matched ${\sim}\num{3.00}\times$ budget,
\emph{topology and width are interchangeable on this corpus}: whatever four
towers with different random initializations discover, a single wider recurrence
already had. Figure~\ref{fig:nexttrack-tower-bank} (left) plots the $N$-axis at
matched capacity, and the line through $\num{186}/\num{170}/\num{172}/\num{186}$
is flat inside the split's own measured resolution
(Section~\ref{sec:nexttrack-power-stats}).

This is also the cleanest demonstration on record of \emph{why the
parameter-matched control leg is mandatory}. Read against the h256 anchor alone,
B4's $+\num{10}$ hits would have looked like a topology gain and the five arms
that lose to C1 would all have read as ties --- the anchor cannot distinguish
``the bank helped'' from ``the extra parameters helped, and the bank spent them
worse than width would have''. The C1 leg does exactly that, and it is what turns
a batch of nine ties into a closure. Note what the flatness costs the \emph{other}
direction: C1's $+\num{10}$ hits over C0 is itself not significant
($+\num{0.006988}$ $[-\num{0.00210},+\num{0.01607}]$), the \textbf{seventh}
parameter-matched width tie on record, so the campaign cannot claim h512 as an
improvement either despite it holding the highest single-GRU hit count measured
(\num{186} against h454's \num{179}). The single-GRU width points on PCA-192 now
read h256\,$\to$\,\num{176}, h297\,$\to$\,\num{162}, h425\,$\to$\,\num{173},
h454\,$\to$\,\num{179}, h512\,$\to$\,\num{186}, h537\,$\to$\,\num{174} ---
non-monotone, spanning \num{0.0168}, with no trend.

\emph{Results, S2.} Table~\ref{tab:nexttrack-towerbank-s2} is the walk. Two arms
--- B2 (\texttt{latent}$+$\texttt{latent} h334) and V3
(\texttt{latent}$+$\texttt{cummean}$+$\texttt{cummean}) --- reached a genuine
\textbf{TRADE} at the primary cell, each buying a drift improvement with the CI
above zero while holding vibe. Both then \textbf{flip} at the deployed cell: the
drift gain evaporates into a straddle and \texttt{vibe} --- the non-negotiable
line --- goes below zero for both. Under the pre-agreed rule, \textbf{no
definition was saved}.

  bought the TRADE decays to a straddle while \texttt{vibe} goes below zero. Grey
  markers are point estimates whose CI straddles (the source report quotes bounds
  only for intervals clearing zero). \textbf{Right:} the campaign's one positive
  result, and it is not about topology --- at byte-identical parameters
  (\num{789696} both, \texttt{model.pt} \num{3162277} bytes both) dropout

\emph{Interpretation.} \textbf{The dose hypothesis is refuted, and it fails in
the direction opposite to the prediction.} Adding fixed-reference
\texttt{cummean} towers did not buy more of what $N=2$ bought; it monotonically
destroyed the walk. Ranked by $\Delta$\texttt{vibe} at the primary cell the
ordering is B4/B2 (all-\texttt{latent}, ${\approx}0$) $>$ B3 $>$ V3 (one
\texttt{cummean}, $-\num{0.0094}$) $>$ V2 ($-\num{0.0144}$, CI below zero) $>$
V1 ($-\num{0.0174}$, CI below zero), and the same ordering holds on S1, where the
three \texttt{cummean}-bearing arms occupy the \emph{bottom three} places
(\num{162}, \num{166}, \num{170} hits) and are the only arms whose
$\Delta$\,music@10 point estimates fall past $-\num{0.005}$. The irony is worth
stating plainly: \textbf{the tower added in order to hold a mood is the tower
that made the walk's mood measure worse} --- the only two arms whose
\texttt{vibe} is refuted outright are the two carrying the most
\texttt{cummean}. Whatever the deployed $N=2$ \texttt{latent}/\texttt{cummean}
engine is doing, ``more \texttt{cummean} is more mood-holding'' is not it, and the
structural reading that motivated the design does not survive being dosed.

\textbf{The sub-hypothesis is confirmed and extends cleanly from $N=2$ to
$N=4$.} B2 (\num{170}), B3 (\num{172}) and B4 (\num{186}) all straddle C0 and
show no trend distinguishable from width noise. This is the same mechanism the
dual-tower scan found (Section~\ref{sec:nexttrack-dualtower}) --- the raw
\texttt{latent} view subsumes its siblings --- now measured at three tower counts
instead of two, which is what closing an axis by \emph{dose} rather than by a
single point requires.

\textbf{The one positive finding is a by-product of the null, and it lands on the
deployed engine.} D2 and R2 differ in \emph{one} hyperparameter: parameters are
byte-identical (\num{789696} both, \texttt{model.pt} \num{3162277} bytes both),
only \texttt{dropout} moves, and $\Delta$recall@10 is $+\num{0.004892}$
$[+\num{0.000699},+\num{0.009783}]$, \textbf{CI above zero} --- dropout
\num{0.1} \emph{beats} dropout \num{0.0} (\num{177} against \num{170} hits).
Read carefully this does not overturn the record's three-times-confirmed
``\texttt{fusion\_layers}${}=0$ beats
\texttt{fusion\_layers}${}=1$'' result; it does something more
awkward. Dropout enters \texttt{seq\_dualgru} only through a pre-MLP, an
inter-layer gap or the fusion MLP, and at \texttt{fusion\_layers}${}=0$ with one
layer and no pre-encoder \emph{all three are absent} --- the f0 path constructs
\textbf{zero} dropout modules. So every f0 arm on record, \textbf{including the
deployed showcase engine}, trained under-regularized and was leaving
${\approx}+\num{0.005}$ recall on the table; and the three recorded f0-vs-f1 gaps
($+\num{0.016}$ / $+\num{0.020}$ / $+\num{0.0266}$,
Table~\ref{tab:nexttrack-fusion-depth}) were measured against an f1 that
\emph{had} regularization its f0 comparator lacked, so they are not like-for-like
and the true topology advantage of f0 is if anything \emph{larger} than recorded.
Wherever those three figures are quoted as clean topology measurements they
should be re-stated as not like-regularized. The cheapest actionable follow-up on
the whole board falls out of this: retrain the deployed engine at
\texttt{dropout}${}=\num{0.1}$ and re-read both surfaces --- a free improvement,
not an architecture bet.

\textbf{The validation-loss dissociation gets a fourth confirmation, now as a
rank correlation} (Figure~\ref{fig:nexttrack-bank-valloss}). Across the ten arms,
Spearman(best validation InfoNCE, hits) $=-\num{0.2954}$, $p=\num{0.407}$ --- no
usable predictive power, and if anything the wrong sign. The sharp instances are
brutal: B4 and B2 differ by \num{0.0003} in validation loss and by \num{16}
hits; V1 holds the fourth-best validation loss in the batch and the \emph{worst}
hit count; C0 holds the \emph{worst} validation loss and beats six of the eight
bank arms; and R2 reaches a better validation loss than D2 while retrieving seven
fewer hits, which makes the dissociation and the dropout finding two faces of one
coin. The decision rule never read validation loss, and this is why.

\begin{figure}[ht]
  \centering
  \includegraphics[width=0.8\textwidth]{nexttrack_bank_valloss}
  \caption{The training objective does not rank retrieval --- fourth
  confirmation, and its sharpest instance yet. Ten arms of the tower-bank batch:
  best validation InfoNCE against hits of \num{1431}. Spearman
  $=-\num{0.2954}$, $p=\num{0.407}$. B4 and B2 are \num{0.0003} apart in
  validation loss and \num{16} hits apart; C0, the worst fit in the batch, beats
  six of the eight banks. Every arm early-stopped (epoch \num{14} seven times,
  then \num{21} and \num{24} twice), so no arm's recall is a lower bound imposed
  by the epoch cap.}
  \label{fig:nexttrack-bank-valloss}
\end{figure}

\textbf{Both walk TRADEs were cell-local, which is a methodological result in its
own right.} B2 and V3 each earned a real $\Delta$drift above zero at
$a\num{0.4}$/$s\num{0.3}$ with vibe held, and both flipped to a vibe below zero
at $a\num{0.8}$/$s\num{0.5}$ with the drift gain gone. Had the design not
pre-registered the secondary-cell requirement, this campaign would have reported
two positive walk results and saved two definitions. \textbf{The secondary-cell
clause is the clause that did the work here}, and it should stay mandatory in
every future walk design. The pre-registered stride probe points the same way: at
$a\num{0.4}$ over $s\in\{\num{0.2},\num{0.3},\num{0.4}\}$ all three
\texttt{cummean} arms peak at $s\num{0.3}$, the cell they were already measured
in --- the third campaign in a row in which re-tuning the stride rescues nothing
--- and at $s\num{0.2}$ their $\Delta$\texttt{vibe} turns positive-straddling
only by taking visibly shorter steps ($\Delta$\texttt{stride\_err} above zero for
all three) and shedding genre variety ($-\num{1.90}$ / $-\num{1.78}$ /
$-\num{1.00}$, all below zero). That is precisely the trade the walk win clause
exists to reject.

\textbf{The failure signature is the opposite of the preceding architecture
campaign's, and that is diagnostic.} No arm here was refuted by a floor: the
recall floor and the relevance floor both pass for all ten runs, and eight of the
nine non-anchor arms sit at \emph{higher} artist concentration than the anchor
(\numrange{0.3547}{0.3799} against \num{0.3559}), i.e.\ the arms did not
de-eager. The floors were therefore not the operative mechanism --- these arms
are not degenerate, they are simply \textbf{flat}. That is a different failure
mode from the immediately preceding depth-in-series campaign
(Section~\ref{sec:nexttrack-stagestack}), in which all five arms fell
\emph{through} the recall floor, and it is why this axis closes as a null rather
than as a harm.

\paragraph{The scope of the closure, precisely.} What closes is
``$N\ge3$ parallel bare-GRU tower banks at \texttt{fusion\_layers}${}=0$'', and
only that, to the standard Section~\ref{sec:nexttrack-stagestack} set for a
closure on this corpus: both surfaces, walk instrument verified. It does
\textbf{not} re-close the dual-tower family, which stays reopened on the walk
surface (Section~\ref{sec:nexttrack-walksurface}) and remains the deployed engine. Nothing
was promoted as champion, no definition was saved, and no dataset was built; the
eight measurement arms were promoted for measurement only, each carrying the
verbatim note ``promoted to enable walk evaluation; not a crown claim'', and the
resulting flooding of the best-models group (to \num{15} entries) was disclosed up
front and handed to the curation agent. Zero of the three pre-authorized
contingency runs fired, and the epoch trace says why: the dropout-confound
extension needed R2 to early-stop later than D2 and both stopped at epoch
\num{14}; the epoch-cap contingency needed an arm to reach the \num{40}-epoch cap
and every arm early-stopped; the width-bracket contingency needed C1 to lose, and
it did not. One limitation is inherited rather than introduced: the walk-surface
reopening this campaign was built to follow up has \emph{no campaign report of its
own} and survives only as a facts roll-up, a git commit and the transcription in
the companion next-track document --- the harness-reproduction gate re-derived all
ten of its recorded means and its deltas exactly, so the \emph{measurement} is
reproducible and the instrument has not moved, but the primary write-up is
missing and should be backfilled.
\emph{Source: \texttt{2026-08-02-tower-bank-parallel-towers.md}.}

\section{Conclusions}
\label{sec:conclusions}
Thirteen campaigns (Jun--Jul 2026) on the pivoted rotation target converge
on a single story, with one late-breaking caveat (point 12). (1) Across two feature campaigns and three model-family scans (two
corpora), every dense modality and every dense distance/margin/dense-net family
loses to categorical identity one-hots read by gradient-boosted trees --- the
song-AE latent dilutes (Section~\ref{sec:ae-blend}), kernel SVM is
worse-than-mean (Section~\ref{sec:svm}), and on the new full-64-d corpus the two
tree families lead while the dense pyramid-MLP is last
(Section~\ref{sec:bakeoff-p64}). (2) The choice of metric is load-bearing on a
binary target: MAE is degenerate (a two-level $\epsilon$-tube predictor posts a
deceptively low MAE while being worse than the mean), and a grid-by-MAE blend
even mis-selects such a predictor; the honest classification metrics (AUC,
log-loss, Brier) restore a fair comparison (Section~\ref{sec:auc}). (3) The
campaigns delivered two measured noise bands --- $2\sigma=0.0086$ MAE and
$2\sigma=0.0126$ AUC --- and the project's first registered, promotable rotation
champion, \texttt{xgboost-classifier-rotation} at AUC 0.7429 on DS-META, plus a
saved new-corpus xgboost definition at AUC 0.7204. (4) The dense neural net was
diagnosed in full (Section~\ref{sec:anns}): an architecture scan refuted the
budget hypothesis (every geometry self-stops at 73--100 iterations and the
converged net reproduces the under-trained AUC to all digits), so the binding
constraint is the raw one-hot \emph{representation}, not optimization --- and the
net's worse-than-random log-loss beside a genuine AUC marks it as confidently
miscalibrated, not merely weak. (5) That diagnosis was then tested directly: the
Rust/\texttt{burn} \texttt{burn-deep-classifier}, which feeds the identity
one-hots \emph{raw}, repaired the calibration completely (log-loss
$2.0\to\sim 0.62$, into the tree regime) and lifted AUC $\sim$2 bands to
\num{0.7038} (Section~\ref{sec:anns-burn}) --- confirming the pathology was a
representation artifact, not an intrinsic property of nets. But the gap to the
trees only \emph{narrowed} (from $\sim$3.3 bands to $\sim$1), it did not close:
the net still lands below \texttt{catboost} and \texttt{xgboost}, the plain
\texttt{mlp} beat both the embedding and wide-deep topologies (entity embeddings
are starved at the corpus's low identity cardinality), and nothing cleared the
$\sim$0.71 save bar. (6) That standing lever was then pulled. A paired re-run on a
\emph{high-vocab} matrix --- identical to the 60/40 dataset but with the artist
cap raised $60\to300$ and genre $40\to120$ --- \emph{flipped} the topology
ranking (Section~\ref{sec:anns-highvocab}): \texttt{embeddings}, starved and
last on the 60/40 matrix, now \emph{beats} \texttt{mlp} (a $+0.0130$ swing that
reverses the prior result and confirms the starvation hypothesis directionally),
and the saved net reaches the in-dataset tree tier at AUC \num{0.7192} (within
$\sim$1 band of \texttt{catboost}) --- the first ANN to clear the rotation save
bar, saved as \texttt{burn-deep-embeddings-highvocab}. The convergent finding
still stands at the top (\texttt{xgboost} leads every matrix, and higher
resolution lifts \emph{every} family, not just the embedding), but the
ANN-pitfalls thread is now closed: a representation-aware net that consumes raw
one-hots is calibrated in the tree regime, and given enough identity resolution
its embedding topology reaches the tree tier --- identity \emph{resolution} was
the lever all along.
(7) A final probe then tested the inverse of the lever --- whether a learned representation \emph{derived from} the tree, rather than fed alongside it, could beat the bare tree --- and refuted it: the Facebook GBDT$\to$LR trick generalized to GBDT$\to$MLP, with a fresh head trained on the frozen champion's one-hot leaf indices, caps \num{1.8}--\num{2.4} bands below the bare xgboost champion (best head AUC \num{0.6978}) and is badly mis-calibrated on every head, because the leaf one-hots are a strictly lossier view of a tree whose additive leaf-value scoring already weights the leaves optimally (Section~\ref{sec:leaf-head}). The dense head cannot even recover the tree that generated its features --- the seventh convergent confirmation that identity resolution, not new heads or representations layered on the trees, is the durable lever.
(8) Finally, the standing identity-resolution lever was mapped to its edge: a vocab-cap scan on the bare GBDT --- frozen champion config, only \texttt{vocab\_top\_n} moved --- shows the lever is \emph{largely spent} (Section~\ref{sec:vocab-cap}). The first cardinality jump ($60/40\to300/120$) carries the gain; past 300/120 every step is sub-band, the curve plateaus inside the noise envelope, xgboost reaches only \num{0.7349} at 1000/300 ($+0.005$, $\sim$0.4 band over the anchor, so no save fired), and \texttt{catboost} tracks it --- two tree families agreeing the marginal high-cardinality columns carry little independent signal. Not saturated and not reversed, but diminishing: raw cap height is no longer the durable lever, and future identity work should target resolution \emph{quality} (follower/popularity interactions, genre hierarchy) rather than bigger caps --- with the full-vocabulary point (6633 cols) still unmeasured against a host memory ceiling.

(9) A tenth campaign characterized a \emph{second} representation-aware net and reached the same ceiling: the two-phase \texttt{burn-ae-classifier}, which pretrains an autoencoder over the assembled feature vector and transplants the encoder into a classifier, is flat across 13 capacity/head configs (a \num{0.0054}-wide strip inside the band), ties the plain \texttt{burn-deep} \texttt{mlp}, and stays $\sim$1.8 bands below the tree champion; freezing the MSE-pretrained encoder under a deep head is a documented collapse mode (Section~\ref{sec:anns-ae}). The unsupervised reconstruction latent is task-agnostic --- a third confirmation that a learned representation downstream of the identity one-hots buys no ranking power the trees lack.
(10) Two interpretability campaigns opened the nets with a per-model sparse autoencoder (Sections~\ref{sec:anns-sae}, \ref{sec:anns-capacity}): at equal AUC, a tapering pyramid \emph{consolidates} its interpretable concepts into a compressed bottleneck while a uniform net keeps a broader set, and at high vocab an AUC-tied embeddings/mlp pair captures the \emph{same} $\sim$0.14-decodable signal with different concept allocation --- ``same signal, different layout,'' invisible to the leaderboard.
(11) A capacity scan on the high-vocab matrix confirmed the dense net is at its ceiling there: neither widening nor deepening beats the $[128,64]$ control by a band, a third embedding layer \emph{hurts} ($-\num{0.0152}$, dilution not starvation), and the best \texttt{mlp} ties the best \texttt{embeddings} --- capacity, not the embeddings mechanism, was the lever, and it is spent (Section~\ref{sec:anns-capacity}).
(12) Finally, the honest forward question was asked --- and it reframes the whole leaderboard. A first-class chronological (train-earlier / test-later) split drops the champion from AUC \num{0.735} to \num{0.619}, a $\sim$0.11 collapse that is \emph{universal} across xgboost, catboost and logistic and is genuine taste non-stationarity, not right-censoring; a walk-forward read lands at \num{0.59}--\num{0.67} every year, never near the random baseline (Section~\ref{sec:drift}). Every on-record AUC is therefore an upper bound on same-era ranking; real forward ``will I replay a song I discover next'' skill is $\sim$0.62--0.63. The mechanism is identity churn --- unseen future artists fall into \texttt{\_\_other\_\_} and the model must lean on the weaker $\sim$0.60 intrinsic floor --- which is precisely why higher vocab caps and bigger nets, both same-era memorization levers, do not transfer forward. This does not overturn any random-split number; it clarifies what they measure and makes richer generalizable intrinsic signal the only remaining lever for forward performance.

(13) A parallel line of work --- \emph{next-track} sequence recommendation, whose task and metrics are unrelated to the rotation boards above (Sections~\ref{sec:nexttrack}--\ref{sec:nexttrack-phase2}) --- was consolidated across twenty-one campaigns and one research memo. A session GRU alone only \emph{ties} the first-order Markov co-listening bar and loses on MRR, and neither loss-tuning nor any topology (depth/width/residual/direction) beats it. But a \emph{rank-fusion} R+M z-blend (GRU $\times$ Markov) beats the bar decisively ($+61\%$ R@10, three-seed confirmed) because the two legs rank \emph{different} correct tracks; a learned XGB stacker underperforms the fixed z-blend and adding redundant neural legs does not help --- the win is signal diversity, not model count. The one genuinely additive new signal is \emph{content}: an item-representation sweep found latent \emph{dimension} (not the compressor) is the lever and peaks at PCA-192, and on that rich space a three-leg R+M+C z-blend is the current best-on-record row at R@10 \num{0.1859}, MRR \num{0.1025} (paired $\Delta$ over the prior champion $+\num{0.0133}$, CI$>$0). Two caveats bound the crown: the reproduction confirm had zero spread because \texttt{seq-nexttrack} is deterministic on this CPU (so it verified determinism, not seed-robustness), and the paired-$\Delta$ lower bound clears zero by only $+\num{0.0007}$ on a single split. Every next-track ranking predictor \emph{was} train-only and off-server until the projection arc: a supervised \emph{learned content projection} (\Rp+M+\Cp) --- an InfoNCE metric map of the PCA-192 item space in which the GRU and content legs retrieve --- then superseded the R+M+C crown at R@10 \num{0.2117} (paired $\Delta$ $+\num{0.0259}$ CI$>$0), resolving both R+M+C caveats (it is seed-robust $\times$3 and cold-reaching) and hardening on a second disjoint 0.64-cold split (paired $\Delta$ $+\num{0.0426}$ CI$>$0, wider on the colder split); it is now \emph{registered, promoted and servable} (\texttt{blend-gru-markov-content-proj}, best-models rank 1, Sections~\ref{sec:nexttrack-projection}--\ref{sec:nexttrack-projection-scan}), closing the productization gap, while every borrowed literature-fit method (session-kNN, rank fusion, learned combiners, Markov smoothing, GRU loss tricks) closed. An item-vector representation exploration then confirmed the equal-weighted PCA-192 geometry handicaps a \emph{raw} model but the fix is \emph{absorbed} by the projection (Section~\ref{sec:nexttrack-representation}), and a continuous ``sounds-alike'' \emph{music@10} metric --- correlated with Recall@10 ($r=\num{0.609}$) but crowning the content-regressing \texttt{seq-nexttrack} as musically-closest --- was added as a display-only column (Section~\ref{sec:nexttrack-music}). Finally, a
measurement campaign added a display-only \emph{session-holisticness} suite
(artist\_adj@10 / mood\_coh@10 / ild@10): the eagerness symptom \emph{tracks}
Recall@10 inside the blend family (the champion is the most artist-eager model at
artist\_adj@10 \num{0.610}) but is sourced from the \emph{Markov} bigram leg, not
the RNN (single GRU / LSTM artist\_adj@10 $\approx$ \num{0.345}), and the GRU and
LSTM are statistically tied (the ``GRU wins because more eager'' hypothesis is
refuted); of two anti-eager levers the eval-time MMR re-rank is the strong knob
($\lambda{=}\num{0.9}$ near-free) and the training-time $\beta$ regularizer is weak,
nothing was promoted, and $\lambda\approx\num{0.9}$ is flagged for a future user
holisticness decision (Section~\ref{sec:nexttrack-holisticness}).

(14) Two closing next-track architecture campaigns then shut the last open
architecture axes, and both are nulls with mechanism. A jointly-trained
\emph{dual-tower} GRU, crossing the complete
$\{$\texttt{latent},\texttt{delta},\texttt{cummean}$\}^2$ view matrix against a
\SI{0.098}{\percent} parameter-matched single tower, fails on all eight arms
(Section~\ref{sec:nexttrack-dualtower}) --- the \emph{fourth} independent
learned-combiner null and the decisive one, because it answers the standing
rebuttal to the earlier three (``of course a bolted-on combiner fails --- train
the fusion end-to-end''): trained end-to-end at the representation level, it
still fails. The mechanism is that the raw \texttt{latent} view already
\emph{subsumes} the others (a GRU over raw latents integrates movement and
running mean internally; the one genuinely complementary pair,
\texttt{delta}$+$\texttt{cummean}, beats both its parents yet is hopeless in
absolute terms). Two by-products are durable: the MLP fusion head is actively
\emph{harmful} (removing it wins in every regime measured, three times over), and
single-GRU width is now closed on PCA-192 as it already was on AE-64. A learned
per-step \emph{pre-encoder} then fails harder
(Section~\ref{sec:nexttrack-preencoder}): the decisive single-tower ablation
\emph{loses} outright ($-\num{0.0140}$, CI below zero, \num{156} hits against
\num{176}), the dose curve is flat to twice the input dimension, and placement is
irrelevant. Because a \emph{linear} pre-encoder is provably expressivity-neutral
here, the rectifier is the only candidate cause --- and a counterfactual
interpretability read confirmed it, measuring the affine map neutral at
${\sim}0.8\sigma$ and the ReLU costing ${\sim}5.9\sigma$, with
\texttt{frac\_exact\_zero} \num{0.5327} matching
\texttt{frac\_negative\_coords} \num{0.5327} to four digits: half-wave
rectification of a zero-centred latent deletes exactly its negative half. Across
five architecture nulls the pattern is hard to dismiss --- adding representation
machinery \emph{around} a single GRU over a good latent space does not help on
this corpus, while every real gain came from improving the latent space itself or
from the fixed z-blend. It is the same shape of finding as the rotation line's
own arc (points 1, 7, 9): learned machinery layered on an already-good
representation buys nothing, and \emph{resolution of the representation} is the
durable lever.
(15) Finally, two facts about the \emph{instrument} rather than any model reframe
the architecture arc (Section~\ref{sec:nexttrack-power}). The nulls are
\textbf{power-limited}: at $n=\num{1431}$ the paired-bootstrap CI half-width is
\num{0.0124} on Recall@10 and ${\sim}\num{0.0018}$ on holisticness@10, while a
whole architecture family spans only \num{0.028} and \num{0.0031} --- so of eight
adjacent arms roughly two tiers are separable on recall and one on holisticness,
and resolving a \num{0.005} difference would need ${\sim}\num{8800}$ sessions.
The honest reading is that \emph{large harms were detected reliably while
moderate wins were never detectable at all}, which is a different claim from
``these architectures do not work'' and yields a prioritization rule: further
architecture arms are a poor use of runs on this split whatever they return,
because the axis's whole dynamic range is barely twice the measurement error.
And \emph{holisticness@10} exists in three mutually non-comparable code
generations (3-factor; 4-factor grounded; grounded plus artist-concentration),
whose differences dwarf every model difference and under all of which the
servable next-track champion ranks \emph{last} --- so the next-track board stays
denominated in Recall@10 and every holisticness column is tagged with its
generation (Table~\ref{tab:nexttrack-hgen}). Note the parallel with point (12):
both lines of work end at a measurement caveat rather than a modelling one ---
rotation's random-split AUC is an upper bound on forward skill, and next-track's
architecture nulls are bounded by test-set size.
(16) A seventh next-track architecture campaign then closed the \emph{parallel}
branch of the axis, and it is the second verdict here to rest on \textbf{two}
evaluation surfaces (point (19) is the first) --- the familiar one-shot top-10,
and the autoregressive
\emph{walk} the showcase actually serves (Section~\ref{sec:nexttrack-towerbank}).
A bank of $N$ parallel bare-GRU towers under one linear head, matched at
\num{3.00}$\times$ the h256 baseline and dosed from $N=2$ to $N=4$, wins on
neither surface: every arm \emph{ties} the baseline on one-shot recall while
\textbf{five of eight lose to a parameter-matched single GRU} with the CI below
zero, and the two arms that earn a walk trade at the primary cell both flip to
\texttt{vibe} below zero at the deployed cell, so no definition was saved. The
decisive datapoint is a zero: four h211 towers (\num{1187700} params) and one
h512 GRU (\num{1182912} params) return the \emph{identical} \num{186} hits of
\num{1431}, paired $\Delta$ \num{0.000000} --- \emph{topology and width are
interchangeable at this budget}, which is also the cleanest demonstration on
record of why the parameter-matched control leg is mandatory: against the anchor
alone, five losing arms would have read as ties. The campaign was built to dose
the walk win's own stated mechanism (a fixed reference never overwritten by
recurrence) and the dose is refuted \emph{ironically} --- the \texttt{cummean}
towers added in order to hold a mood are the worst arms in the batch on both
surfaces and the only two whose walk \texttt{vibe} is refuted outright. The one
CI-clear result in the whole batch is a by-product about \emph{regularization},
not topology: at byte-identical parameters dropout \num{0.1} beats dropout
\num{0.0} by $+\num{0.0049}$ recall@10. That is consequential because the
\texttt{fusion\_layers}${}=0$ path constructs \emph{zero} dropout modules, so
every f0 arm on record --- including the deployed showcase engine --- trained
under-regularized, the three recorded f0-vs-f1 gaps are not like-regularized, and
the cheapest improvement on the board is a single retrain. It is the same shape as
points (7), (9) and (14): machinery layered on an already-good representation
buys nothing, and the durable levers are the representation and the training
regime.

(17) Six further next-track campaigns then complete that line, and the two
\emph{positive} results in them are both score-time changes rather than models.
The eval-time MMR $\lambda$ frontier (Section~\ref{sec:nexttrack-mmr}) is the first
confirmed holisticness lever on record --- eight of eight arms lift it with the CI
above zero, three single-GRU arms clear the prior all-time bar at a recall tie, and
three of the four crown factors improve together --- and it resolves its
pre-registered mechanism question unanimously: sonic diversification
de-concentrates \emph{artists} $\numrange{1.59}{3.06}\times$ faster than it reduces
seed-echo in 8 of 8 arms, so the explicit artist-penalty sequel is cancelled. Its
by-product is a measurement rule: shared-weights pairing is
$\numrange{3}{33}\times$ sharper than the architecture-family noise floor of point
(15), so eval-time levers must budget their minimum detectable effect from the
pairing. Then \texttt{markov\_gate}
(Section~\ref{sec:nexttrack-markovgate}) delivers the largest single recall gain in
the whole arc from nine lines and zero new parameters: making the Markov leg
\emph{abstain} where the last prefix item has no train-bigram support
(\SI{55.6}{\percent} of queries) lifts Recall@10 to \num{0.23201} (\num{332} of
\num{1431}), paired $+\num{0.02027}$ canonically and $+\num{0.01957}$ on a disjoint
\SI{64}{\percent}-cold split. The defect is the blend's \emph{scale-invariant}
normalizer re-inflating an evidence-free back-off to a full one-third vote, and the
bucket decomposition --- whole effect in the zero-support bucket, both warm buckets
\textbf{byte-for-byte identical} --- mechanically proves that no global static leg
weight could have found it, which \emph{narrows} the four-times-confirmed
learned-combiner pitfall to \emph{learned score-level fusion} rather than per-query
evidence gating. It is a recommendation, not a promotion.
(18) The negative results in the same window are equally load-bearing. The
training-time anti-eager regularizer is refuted \emph{expressively}, not
dosimetrically (Section~\ref{sec:nexttrack-eagermargin}): its margins were
calibrated from the item$\to$item transition tail while the hinge acts on
prediction$\to$current, whose ceiling lies below the quantiles used, so four arms
reproduce the control's loss to \num{16} significant digits and the arms that do
fire destroy relevance in the same motion that de-eagers. What \emph{does} work is
the first lever in this project that cannot be outscored: a \textbf{hard per-artist
cap} on the top-$k$ (Section~\ref{sec:nexttrack-artistcap}), which takes
\texttt{artist\_conc@10} \num{0.692}$\to$\num{0.113} and generation-3
holisticness@10 \num{0.0086}$\to$\num{0.0481} while \texttt{music@10} \emph{rises}
with the CI above zero at every setting on both models --- it trades \emph{literal}
relevance for \emph{graded} relevance --- hardened on a disjoint
\SI{64}{\percent}-cold split on two pre-registered legs, at a stated price of
\num{52} of \num{1431} exact hits. A purpose-built rollout metric
(Section~\ref{sec:nexttrack-rollout}) returns a sixth architecture null in the
deployment regime and finds per-seed variance dominating model identity; and a
research memo (Section~\ref{sec:nexttrack-memo}), reported at a lower register
because nothing in it was run on the server, converges from five independent
directions on the thesis that every surviving proposal is an
\emph{evidence-conditioning} change rather than a capacity change --- the same shape
as points (1), (7), (9) and (14) on the rotation side.
(19) A stage-stack depth campaign (Section~\ref{sec:nexttrack-stagestack}) finally
closes the \emph{serial} branch of the architecture axis on \emph{both} evaluation
surfaces, the standard the tower-bank campaign of point (16) then inherits. Five
capacity-equalised arrangements of per-step MLP and recurrent stages all fall
\emph{through} the mandatory recall floor and are refuted again on the walk, while a
$\num{3.24}\times$ capacity control ties on both --- so the damage is depth itself,
and not capacity, regularization or the MLP (a leading per-step MLP is
recall-neutral and holisticness-positive at depth 4, which narrows the $N=1$
pre-encoder result of point (14) to the shallow case). Two facts outlive the null:
bracketing beats interleaving at byte-identical capacity ($+\num{0.01537}$, CI
above zero), and the training objective \textbf{dissociates} from retrieval --- the
arm with the best validation InfoNCE in the batch retrieves \num{55} fewer hits
than the control, so any architecture search on this family that selects on
validation loss selects the wrong arm.

\section{Source reports}
This synthesis consolidates thirty-five source documents from \texttt{experiments/}
--- thirty-four campaign reports and one research memo, the latter flagged as such
because nothing in it was run on the server:
\begin{itemize}
  \item \texttt{2026-06-09-ae-xgboost-blend.md} --- autoencoder-latent +
    XGBoost blend on rotation (Section~\ref{sec:ae-blend}).
  \item \texttt{2026-06-09-svm-kernel-rotation.md} --- kernel SVM scan and
    conditional blend; first measured MAE noise band (Section~\ref{sec:svm}).
  \item \texttt{2026-06-09-rotation-classification.md} --- AUC bake-off, first
    measured AUC noise band, first registered rotation champion
    (Section~\ref{sec:auc}).
  \item \texttt{2026-06-12-classifier-bakeoff-p64.md} --- four-family
    classifier bake-off and xgboost depth/lr sweep on the new full-64-d corpus
    (Sections~\ref{sec:bakeoff-p64} and~\ref{sec:anns}).
  \item \texttt{2026-06-12-pyramid-mlp-arch-scan.md} --- six-geometry
    pyramid-MLP architecture scan, refuting the budget hypothesis
    (Sections~\ref{sec:mlp-scan} and~\ref{sec:anns}).
  \item \texttt{2026-06-12-burn-deep-topology.md} --- the Rust/\texttt{burn}
    \texttt{burn-deep-classifier} topology bake-off; resolves the
    representation-aware-net question, fixing calibration but not the AUC gap
    (Section~\ref{sec:anns-burn}).
  \item \texttt{2026-06-12-high-vocab-topology.md} --- the paired topology
    re-run on a higher-identity-cardinality matrix; flips
    \texttt{embeddings}$>$\texttt{mlp} and lifts the saved net into the
    in-dataset tree tier, the first ANN to clear the rotation save bar
    (Section~\ref{sec:anns-highvocab}).
  \item \texttt{2026-06-15-gbdt-leaf-head-ceiling-probe.md} --- the Facebook
    GBDT$\to$LR trick generalized to GBDT$\to$MLP; every leaf-embedding head
    caps below the bare xgboost champion with broken calibration, the seventh
    convergent refutation (Section~\ref{sec:leaf-head}).
  \item \texttt{2026-06-15-vocab-cap-scan-gbdt.md} --- the identity-resolution vocab-cap scan on the bare GBDT; maps the cap$\to$AUC curve and finds the lever largely spent past 300/120 (sub-band gains, no save fired), with the all-vocab point unmeasured at the host memory ceiling (Section~\ref{sec:vocab-cap}).
  \item \texttt{2026-06-15-burn-ae-classifier-capacity-scan.md} --- first
    characterization of the two-phase \texttt{burn-ae-classifier}; 13 configs in
    one sub-band strip, ties the plain \texttt{burn-deep} \texttt{mlp}, and a
    frozen-deep-head collapse mode (Section~\ref{sec:anns-ae}).
  \item \texttt{2026-07-12-pyramid-vs-rect-sae.md} --- per-model SAE
    representation read; at equal AUC the pyramid consolidates concepts into its
    bottleneck while the uniform net keeps a broader set
    (Section~\ref{sec:anns-sae}).
  \item \texttt{2026-07-12-high-vocab-capacity-scan.md} --- the
    \texttt{burn-deep-classifier} capacity scan on the high-vocab matrix
    (CEILING) plus a cross-family SAE allocation read
    (Section~\ref{sec:anns-capacity}).
  \item \texttt{2026-07-12-taste-drift-temporal-split.md} --- the chronological
    versus random split; a universal $\sim$0.11 AUC drop reframing the
    leaderboard as an upper bound on same-era ranking (Section~\ref{sec:drift}).
  \item \texttt{2026-07-15-nexttrack-compression-representation-bakeoff.md} ---
    the first \emph{next-track} sequence-recommendation campaign folded in: an
    item-representation compression bake-off (PCA vs autoencoder $\times$ latent
    dim 32/64/128) under a frozen GRU readout. PCA-128 is the single-GRU winner
    (paired-$\Delta$ $+0.0189$, CI$>$0) but the win does not carry into the R+M
    blend, so nothing was promoted (Section~\ref{sec:nexttrack-compression}).
  \item \texttt{2026-07-13-nexttrack-sequence-sweep.md} --- the founding
    next-track campaign: a GRU/LSTM sequence sweep against the first-order
    Markov bar; the best GRU-infonce ties on R@10 but loses on MRR
    (Section~\ref{sec:nexttrack-sweep}).
  \item \texttt{2026-07-13-nexttrack-topology-scan.md} --- depth / width /
    residual / bidirectional topology scan; architecture is a spent lever, all
    arms cap below the Markov bar (Section~\ref{sec:nexttrack-topology}).
  \item \texttt{2026-07-14-nexttrack-blend-and-ensemble.md} --- the
    GRU$\times$Markov R+M z-blend beats the bar $+61\%$ R@10 (the first
    next-track champion); no richer ensemble improves on it and the learned XGB
    stacker underperforms (Section~\ref{sec:nexttrack-blend}).
  \item \texttt{2026-07-14-nexttrack-graded-relevance-and-new-legs.md} ---
    first-class graded-relevance metrics (artist@k / genre@k / artist\_mrr) and
    new leak-free base learners; content-kNN is the only additive signal, tying
    the champion on AE-64 (Section~\ref{sec:nexttrack-graded}).
  \item \texttt{2026-07-15b-nexttrack-compression-dim-extension.md} --- the
    PCA/AE dimension sweep extended to 192/256; the curve peaks at PCA-192 and
    turns over, so PCA-128 stays the Phase-2 base (Section~\ref{sec:nexttrack-dim}).
  \item \texttt{2026-07-15c-nexttrack-phase2-model-sweep.md} --- the
    model-family sweep on the rich PCA spaces; the fixed z-blend beats the
    learned stacker, LSTM is the best single model, and R+M+C on PCA-192 is
    crowned the new best-on-record row (Section~\ref{sec:nexttrack-modelsweep}).
  \item \texttt{2026-07-18-nexttrack-literature-fit-campaign.md} --- a
    bibliography-driven exploration; a supervised learned content projection
    (\Rp+M+\Cp) is a new best-on-record row (R@10 \num{0.2117}, paired $\Delta$ over
    R+M+C $+\num{0.0259}$ CI$>$0, seed-robust, cold-reaching), and every borrowed
    method (session-kNN, rank fusion, learned combiner, Markov smoothing, GRU
    importance negatives) closes (Section~\ref{sec:nexttrack-projection-win}).
  \item \texttt{2026-07-18b-nexttrack-projection-registration-and-scan.md} --- the
    projection reproduced on-server, registered $+$ promoted $+$ servable as
    \texttt{blend-gru-markov-content-proj} (best-models rank 1); an objective / rank
    / $\tau$ scan (InfoNCE $\gg$ BPR; default held) and second-split hardening on a
    disjoint 0.64-cold split (paired $\Delta$ $+\num{0.0426}$ CI$>$0)
    (Section~\ref{sec:nexttrack-projection-scan}).
  \item \texttt{2026-07-18c-nexttrack-representation-exploration.md} --- an
    item-vector representation audit; the equal-weighted PCA-192 geometry handicaps
    the raw GRU (six of eight alt spaces lift it CI$>$0) but the gain is absorbed by
    the projection (Section~\ref{sec:nexttrack-representation}).
  \item \texttt{2026-07-20-musical-distance-metric.md} --- the continuous
    ``sounds-alike'' music@10 metric and its 53-run offline backfill; it correlates
    with Recall@10 ($r=\num{0.609}$) but crowns a different winner, the
    content-regressing \texttt{seq-nexttrack} (Section~\ref{sec:nexttrack-music}).
  \item \texttt{2026-07-22-session-holisticness-and-antieager-levers.md} ---
    a display-only session-holisticness metric suite (artist\_adj@10 /
    mood\_coh@10 / ild@10) and two anti-eager levers (eval-time MMR
    \texttt{mmr\_lambda}, training-time \texttt{eager\_beta}); the champion is
    the most artist-eager model, the eagerness is sourced from the Markov leg,
    GRU\,$\approx$\,LSTM, and MMR $\lambda$ is the strong knob
    (Section~\ref{sec:nexttrack-holisticness}).
  \item \texttt{2026-07-25-nexttrack-dual-tower-fusion-scan.md} --- the
    dual-tower view-pairing $\times$ fusion-depth scan against a
    \SI{0.098}{\percent} parameter-matched single tower; \textbf{REFUTED} (the
    fourth learned-combiner null, closing the ``train the fusion end-to-end''
    rebuttal), with the raw-latent view shown to subsume
    \texttt{delta}/\texttt{cummean}, the MLP fusion head shown actively harmful,
    single-GRU width closed on PCA-192, and holisticness/recall shown
    anti-correlated across the batch (Section~\ref{sec:nexttrack-dualtower}).
  \item \texttt{2026-07-26-nexttrack-pre-encoder-scan.md} --- the learned
    per-step pre-encoder scan (presence $\times$ placement $\times$ dose) with the
    decisive single-tower ablation; \textbf{REFUTED and actively harmful}
    ($-\num{0.0140}$ Recall@10, CI below zero), the rectifier identified
    analytically and then measured (affine map neutral at ${\sim}0.8\sigma$, ReLU
    ${\sim}5.9\sigma$, \texttt{frac\_exact\_zero} $=$
    \texttt{frac\_negative\_coords} $=$ \num{0.5327}), plus the measured power
    analysis and the three holisticness generations
    (Sections~\ref{sec:nexttrack-preencoder} and~\ref{sec:nexttrack-power}).
  \item \texttt{2026-07-26-nexttrack-mmr-lambda-frontier.md} --- the native
    eval-time MMR $\lambda$ frontier on both anchors; \textbf{CONFIRMED} as the
    first genuine holisticness lever on record (three TIER-1 ``free'' single-GRU
    arms above the prior all-time bar at a recall tie, best $H$ \num{0.030355}),
    with the pre-registered mechanism question resolved unanimously to the
    responsive hypothesis (8 of 8 arms, ratio \numrange{1.59}{3.06}), the binding
    facet shown to switch as $\lambda$ falls, the candidate-pool axis closed as a
    fine-tuning knob, and the shared-weights pairing measured
    $\numrange{3}{33}\times$ sharper than the architecture-family noise floor
    (Section~\ref{sec:nexttrack-mmr}).
  \item \texttt{2026-07-27-rollout-hold-metric.md} --- \texttt{hold@}$N$, a
    generation metric for the autoregressive rollout regime (per-axis fade
    calibrated on \num{2135} real train sessions, with the anti-gaming term inside
    the metric), plus five sweeps and \num{240} journeys; \textbf{REFUTED} as a
    model discriminator --- a sixth architecture null, obtained in the deployment
    regime --- with per-seed variance shown to dominate model identity and three
    retracted positives documented as a catalogue of generation-metric pitfalls
    (Section~\ref{sec:nexttrack-rollout}).
  \item \texttt{2026-07-27b-nexttrack-model-topology-research-memo.md} ---
    \textbf{a research memo, not a campaign report}: nothing was run on the
    server, nothing promoted, nothing registered, and its numbers come from
    offline screens over frozen checkpoints. Consolidated at a lower register,
    with its own verified/provisional partition preserved and its test-set
    screening caveat stated; its reusable content is the convergent thesis that
    every surviving proposal is an \emph{evidence-conditioning} change rather than
    a capacity change, the mechanical explanation of the learned-combiner anomaly,
    and the graveyard (Section~\ref{sec:nexttrack-memo}).
  \item \texttt{2026-07-27c-nexttrack-markov-gate-confirm.md} --- the native
    two-split confirm of \texttt{markov\_gate}; \textbf{CONFIRMED} and the largest
    single recall gain in the arc (R@10 \num{0.23201} $=$ \num{332} of \num{1431},
    paired $+\num{0.02027}$ canonically and $+\num{0.01957}$ on a disjoint
    \SI{64}{\percent}-cold split, MRR up on both), with the warm evidence buckets
    \emph{byte-for-byte identical}, an unpredicted crown gain in which all four
    factors move the right way at once, a first recall-neutral crown arm when
    composed with MMR, and the learned-combiner pitfall narrowed accordingly
    (Section~\ref{sec:nexttrack-markovgate}).
  \item \texttt{2026-07-30-eager-margin-calibrated-refutation.md} --- the
    training-time eagerness regularizer at margins calibrated from the measured
    transition tail; \textbf{REFUTED across the whole axis, with a mechanism}: the
    margins were drawn from the item$\to$item cosine while the hinge acts on
    prediction$\to$current, whose ceiling sits between \num{0.5843} and \num{0.70},
    so four arms are provably inert (loss traces bit-identical at $\beta$ up to the
    schema cap) and the arms that fire destroy relevance before they help
    (Section~\ref{sec:nexttrack-eagermargin}).
  \item \texttt{2026-07-30c-artist-cap-measurement.md} --- the \emph{hard
    per-artist cap}, the first de-eagering lever that works, and it works because
    it cannot be outscored: \texttt{artist\_conc@10}
    \num{0.692}$\to$\num{0.113} with \texttt{music@10} \emph{rising} (CI above
    zero) at every setting on both models, hardened on a disjoint
    \SI{64}{\percent}-cold split on two pre-registered legs, at a stated price of
    \num{52} of \num{1431} exact hits; it is also where this document introduces
    the second evaluation surface, the autoregressive walk
    (Section~\ref{sec:nexttrack-artistcap}).
  \item \texttt{2026-08-01-stage-stack-topology.md} --- the stage-stack
    topology campaign (arrangements of per-step MLP $\times$ recurrence at
    equalised capacity, via the new \texttt{seq-stack} predictor);
    \textbf{REFUTED on both surfaces} and the first closure on this corpus to that
    standard: all five 4-stage arms fall \emph{through} the recall floor and are
    refuted again on the walk, the $\num{3.24}\times$ capacity control ties on
    both, the user's rectifier-position hypothesis is refuted, bracketing beats
    interleaving at byte-identical capacity, and the best-fitting arm in the batch
    retrieves \num{55} fewer hits than the control
    (Section~\ref{sec:nexttrack-stagestack}).
  \item \texttt{2026-08-02-tower-bank-parallel-towers.md} --- the
    parallel-tower-bank dose campaign ($N$ bare-GRU towers under one linear head,
    via the new \texttt{seq-bank} predictor) and the first campaign here judged on
    \emph{two} surfaces; \textbf{CLOSED on both}: no arm wins, five of eight lose
    to a parameter-matched single GRU, four h211 towers and one h512 GRU return the
    identical \num{186} hits (paired $\Delta$ \num{0.000000}), the
    \texttt{cummean} dose makes the walk strictly worse, both walk trades are
    cell-local, and the batch's one CI-clear result is that dropout \num{0.1}
    beats dropout \num{0.0} at byte-identical parameters --- which implies every
    \texttt{fusion\_layers}${}=0$ arm on record, the deployed engine included,
    trained under-regularized (Section~\ref{sec:nexttrack-towerbank}).
\end{itemize}
